## Regulations Made Under Part II of the Canada Labour Code Respecting Occupational Safety and Health of Employees Employed on or in Connection with Exploration or Drilling for, or the Production, Conservation, Processing or Transportation of, Oil or Gas in Canada Lands, as Defined in the Canada Oil and Gas Act {#regulations-made-under-part-ii-of-the-canada-labour-code-respecting-occupational-safety-and-health-of-employees-employed-on-or-in-connection-with-exploration-or-drilling-for-or-the-production-conservation-processing-or-transportation-of-oil-or-gas-in-canada-lands-as-defined-in-the-canada-oil-and-gas-act .Title-of-Act}

[SOR]{.abbr title="Statutory Orders and Regulations"}/87-612
:::

[CANADA LABOUR CODE](/eng/acts/L-2)

Registration 1987-10-22

Regulations Made Under Part II of the Canada Labour Code Respecting
Occupational Safety and Health of Employees Employed on or in Connection
with Exploration or Drilling for, or the Production, Conservation,
Processing or Transportation of, Oil or Gas in Canada Lands, as Defined
in the Canada Oil and Gas Act

P.C. 1987-2169 1987-10-22
::::

:::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: {.section .order}
Her Excellency the Governor General in Council, on the recommendation of
the Minister of Labour, the Minister of Energy, Mines and Resources and
the Minister of Indians Affairs and Northern Development, pursuant to
sections 82[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#fn_3856_hq_7930){#fn_3856_hq_7930-ID0EJBAA} and
83[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#fn_3856_hq_7930){#fn_3856_hq_7930-ID0EHBAA},
subsection 106(1)[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#fn_3856_hq_7930){#fn_3856_hq_7930-ID0EFBAA} and
paragraph 106(3)(b)[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#fn_3856_hq_7930){#fn_3856_hq_7930-ID0EDBAA} of
the Canada Labour Code, is pleased hereby to make the annexed
Regulations made under Part IV of the [Canada Labour
Code](/eng/acts/L-2) respecting occupational safety and health of
employees employed on or in connection with exploration or drilling for
or the production, conservation, processing or transportation of oil or
gas in Canada lands, as defined in the Canada Oil and Gas Act, effective
October 30, 1987.

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#fn_3856_hq_7930-ID0EJBAA){#fn_3856_hq_7930}S.C.
  1984, c. 39, s. 20
  :::
:::

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/2025-79,
s. 18\]]{.Repealed}

## [PART I]{.HLabel1}[General]{.HTitleText1} {#h-908064 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908065 .Subheading}

**[[1.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1.1 .sectionLabel}** In these Regulations,

[[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means Part II of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2); ([Loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[advanced first aid certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[advanced first aid certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
    certificate issued by an approved organization for the successful
    completion of a first aid course of at least five days' duration,
    other than a mariners' first aid course; ([certificat de secourisme
    avancé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[ANSI]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ANSI]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the American National Standards
    Institute; ([ANSI]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[API]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[API]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the American Petroleum Institute;
    ([API]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[approved organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[approved organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the St. John
    Ambulance, the Canadian Red Cross Society or the Workers'
    Compensation Board of British Columbia; ([organisme
    approuvé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[ASME]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ASME]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the American Society of
    Mechanical Engineers; ([ASME]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[basket]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[basket]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a personnel transfer basket;
    ([nacelle]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Canadian Electrical Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Canadian Electrical Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means CSA Standard
    C22.1-1990 Canadian Electrical Code, Part I, dated January 1990;
    ([Code canadien de l'électricité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[CPR course]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[CPR course]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a training course in
    cardiopulmonary resuscitation based on the publication of the
    Journal of the American Medical Association entitled Standards and
    Guidelines for Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation and Emergency Cardiac
    Care, dated June 6, 1986, as reprinted by the American Heart
    Association; ([cours RCP]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[CSA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[CSA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canadian Standards
    Association; ([CSA]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[dangerous substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[dangerous substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
    SOR/88-199, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

[[drill floor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[drill floor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a drilling
    rig or drilling unit, the stable platform surrounding the slip
    setting area that provides support for employees during drilling
    operations; ([plancher de forage]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[drilling rig]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[drilling rig]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the plant and associated
    support equipment used to make a hole or well by boring or other
    means for geophysical, exploration or production purposes;
    ([appareil de forage]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[drilling unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[drilling unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a drillship,
    submersible, semi-submersible, barge, jack-up or other vessel used
    in drilling and includes a drilling rig and other related
    facilities; ([installation de forage]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[electrical equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[electrical equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means equipment for the
    generation, distribution or use of electricity; ([outillage
    électrique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[elevating device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[elevating device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an escalator,
    elevator, basket or other device for moving passengers or freight;
    ([appareil de levage]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[emergency first aid certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[emergency first aid certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
    certificate issued by an approved organization for the successful
    completion of a first aid course of at least one day's duration;
    ([certificat de secourisme d'urgence]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[environmental conditions]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[environmental conditions]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
    meteorological, oceanographical and other natural conditions,
    including ice conditions, that may affect the operations of a work
    place; ([conditions environnementales]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[field accommodation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[field accommodation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means living, eating or
    sleeping quarters provided by an employer for the accommodation of
    employees at a work place; ([logement sur place]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[fire hazard area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fire hazard area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area that contains
    or is likely to contain explosive or flammable concentrations of a
    hazardous substance; ([endroit présentant un risque
    d'incendie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[first aid attendant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[first aid attendant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a medic or a
    qualified person who is a holder of an emergency first aid
    certificate, a standard first aid certificate, a mariners first aid
    certificate or an advanced first aid certificate or of a registered
    nurse's certificate recognized under the laws of a province;
    ([secouriste]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[first aid room]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[first aid room]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a room used exclusively
    for first aid or medical purposes; ([salle de premiers
    soins]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[high voltage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[high voltage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a voltage of more than
    750 V between any two conductors or between a conductor and ground;
    ([haute tension]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[hot work]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[hot work]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means welding, burning, rivetting,
    drilling, grinding, chipping or any other work where a flame is used
    or sparks are produced; ([travail à chaud]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[locked out]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[locked out]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of any
    equipment, machine or device, that the equipment, machine or device
    has been rendered inoperative and cannot be operated or energized
    without the consent of the person who rendered it inoperative;
    ([verrouillé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[mariners first aid certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[mariners first aid certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
    certificate issued by an approved organization for the successful
    completion of a mariners' first aid course of at least five days'
    duration; ([certificat de secourisme maritime]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[medic]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[medic]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a qualified person who

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has experience with helicopter or fixed-wing
      aircraft evacuation for medical purposes,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is the holder of an advanced cardiac life support
      certificate or basic cardiac life support instructor's certificate
      recognized by the Canadian Heart Foundation, and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is the holder of

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a registered nurse's certificate recognized
        under the laws of a province and has clinical experience in
        intensive care or emergency practice,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a paramedic certificate issued by a college in
        a province and has clinical experience, or

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} an Occupational Qualification VIB Medical
        Assistant Canadian military certificate; ([technicien
        médical]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[medical practitioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[medical practitioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
    SOR/88-199, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

[[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, SOR/2021-118, s.
    8\]]{.Repealed}

[[National Building Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[National Building Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the National
    Building Code of Canada, 1985, issued by the Associate Committee on
    the National Building Code, National Research Council of Canada,
    dated 1985, as amended to January 1987; ([Code national du
    bâtiment]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[National Fire Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[National Fire Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the National Fire
    Code of Canada, 1985, issued by the Associate Committee on the
    National Fire Code, National Research Council of Canada, dated 1985,
    as amended to January 1987; ([Code national de prévention des
    incendies]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[offshore]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[offshore]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, with respect to a work
    place, a location within a water-covered area that is not an island,
    an artificial island or an ice platform; ([au large des
    côtes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[oxygen deficient atmosphere]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[oxygen deficient atmosphere]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
    atmosphere in which there is less than 18 per cent by volume of
    oxygen at a pressure of one atmosphere or in which the partial
    pressure of oxygen is less than 135 mm Hg; ([air à faible teneur en
    oxygène]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[production facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[production facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means production,
    separating, treating and processing equipment and facilities
    necessary in production operations, including airstrips, helicopter
    landing areas and field accommodation; ([installation de
    production]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[protection equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[protection equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means safety materials,
    equipment, devices and clothing; ([équipement de
    protection]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[qualified person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[qualified person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a
    specified duty, a person who, because of his knowledge, training and
    experience, is qualified to perform that duty safely and properly;
    ([personne qualifiée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[readily available]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[readily available]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a
    document, present and easily accessible at the work place at all
    times; ([facilement accessible]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[regional office]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[regional office]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a work
    place, the regional office of the Canada Oil and Gas Lands
    Administration, formed under the Department of Energy, Mines and
    Resources and the Department of Indian Affairs and Northern
    Development, for the administrative region of that Administration in
    which the work place is situated; ([bureau
    régional]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[standard first aid certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[standard first aid certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
    certificate issued by an approved organization for successful
    completion of a first aid course of at least two days' duration;
    ([certificat de secourisme général]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[support craft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[support craft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vehicle, vessel, tug,
    ship, aircraft, air cushion vehicle, standby craft or other craft
    used to provide transport for or assistance to employees in a work
    place; ([véhicule de service]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[toilet room]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[toilet room]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a room that contains a
    water closet or a urinal, but does not include an outdoor privy;
    ([cabinet de toilette]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[ULC Standard]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ULC Standard]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Underwriters'
    Laboratories of Canada Standard CAN4-S508-M83, Rating and Fire
    Testing of Fire Extinguishers, dated June 1983, as amended to
    July 1986. ([norme ULC]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, ss. 1, 19
- SOR/94-165, s. 3
- SOR/2014-141, s. 14(F)
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 8](#1295160-1312173){.wb-lbx

### [Prescription]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908116 .Subheading}

**[[1.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1.2 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations are
prescribed for the purposes of sections 125, 125.1, 125.2 and 126 of the
Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 2
- SOR/94-165, s. 4
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908120 .Subheading}

**[[1.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1.21 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations
apply to any person who is not an employee but who performs for an
employer to which these Regulations apply activities whose primary
purpose is to enable the person to acquire knowledge or experience, and
to the employer, as if that person were an employee of the employer, and
every provision of these Regulations must be read accordingly.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-211, s. 3
:::

- **[[1.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), these
  Regulations, other than Part II, apply in respect of employees
  employed on or in connection with exploration or drilling for or the
  production, conservation, processing or transportation, other than
  transportation through an interprovincial pipeline, of oil or gas in
  Canada lands, as defined in the Canada Oil and Gas Act.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), Part II applies in respect
  of employees employed in Canada lands, as defined in the Canada Oil
  and Gas Act, on or in connection with the transportation of oil or gas
  through an interprovincial pipeline.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} These Regulations do not apply in respect of
  employees employed in the operation of ships or aircraft.

### [Records and Reports]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908128 .Subheading}

**[[1.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1.4 .sectionLabel}** If an employer is
required to keep and maintain a record, report or other document
referred to in section 125 or 125.1 of the Act, the employer shall keep
and maintain the record, report or other document in such a manner that
it is readily available for examination by the Head of Compliance and
Enforcement and by the safety and health committee or the safety and
health representative, if either exists, for the work place to which it
applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 3
- SOR/94-165, s. 5
- SOR/2014-148, s. 22
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 9](#1295161-1312175){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295161-1312175.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-1.4-20141031.html "Link to previous version of section 1.4"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Inconsistent Provisions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908132 .Subheading}

**[[1.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1.5 .sectionLabel}** In the event of an
inconsistency between any standard incorporated by reference in these
Regulations and any other provision of these Regulations, that other
provision of these Regulations shall prevail to the extent of the
inconsistency.

**[[1.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1.6 .sectionLabel}** Despite any provision
in any standard incorporated by reference in these Regulations, other
than a standard incorporated in section 13.7, a reference to another
publication in that standard is a reference to the publication as it
read on October 30, 1987.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 8](#1343832-1344456){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343832-1344456.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-1.6-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 1.6"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART II]{.HLabel1}[Interprovincial Pipelines]{.HTitleText1} {#h-908135 .Part}

**[[2.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2.1 .sectionLabel}** The Canada Occupational
Safety and Health Regulations apply in respect of employees employed in
Canada lands, as defined in the Canada Oil and Gas Act, on or in
connection with the transportation of oil or gas through an
interprovincial pipeline.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 6(F)
:::

## [PART III]{.HLabel1}[Building Safety]{.HTitleText1} {#h-908139 .Part}

### [Standards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908140 .Subheading}

**[[3.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.1 .sectionLabel}** The design and
construction of every building on shore shall meet the standards set out
in Parts 3 to 9 of the National Building Code so far as is reasonably
practicable.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 7(F)
:::

### [Doors]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908144 .Subheading}

**[[3.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.2 .sectionLabel}** Every double action
swinging door that is located in an exit, entrance or passageway used
for two-way pedestrian traffic shall be designed and fitted in a manner
that will permit persons who are approaching from one side of the door
to be aware of persons who are on the other side of the door.

### [Floor and Wall Openings]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908146 .Subheading}

- **[[3.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.3 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section,

  [[floor opening]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[floor opening]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an opening measuring
      300 mm or more in its smallest dimension in a floor, platform,
      pavement or yard; ([ouverture dans un plancher]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[wall opening]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[wall opening]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an opening at least 750
      mm high and 300 mm wide in a wall or partition. ([ouverture dans
      un mur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an employee has access to a wall opening from
  which there is a drop of more than 1.2 m or to a floor opening,
  guardrails shall be fitted around the wall opening or floor opening or
  the opening shall be covered with material capable of supporting all
  loads that may be imposed on it.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The material referred to in subsection (2) shall be
  securely fastened to a supporting structural member of the building.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply to the loading and
  unloading areas of truck, railroad and marine docks.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 3.10, guardrails shall be
  installed around the perimeter of every work place, other than a
  helicopter deck, where there is a drop of more than 1 m from the work
  place to an adjacent area.

### [Open Top Bins, Hoppers, Vats and Pits]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908155 .Subheading}

- **[[3.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an employee has access to an
  open top bin, hopper, vat, pit or other open top enclosure from a
  point directly above the enclosure, the enclosure shall be fitted with
  a fixed ladder on the inside wall of the enclosure and shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} covered with a grating, screen or other covering
    that will prevent the employee from falling into the enclosure; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provided with a walkway that is not less than 500
    mm wide and is fitted with guardrails.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A grating, screen, covering or walkway referred to in
  subsection (1) shall be so designed, constructed and maintained that
  it will support a load that is not less than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the maximum load that is likely to be imposed on
    it, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a live load of 6 kPa,

  whichever is the greater.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 8(E)
:::

### [Ladders, Stairways and Ramps]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908166 .Subheading}

**[[3.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.5 .sectionLabel}** Where an employee in
the course of employment is required to move from one level to another
level that is more than 450 mm higher or lower than the former level,
the employer shall install a fixed ladder, stairway or ramp between the
levels.

**[[3.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.6 .sectionLabel}** Where one end of a
stairway is so close to a traffic route used by vehicles, to a machine
or to any other hazard as to be hazardous to the safety of an employee
using the stairway, the employer shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where practicable, install a barricade that will
  protect employees using the stairway from the hazard; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where it is not reasonably practicable to install a
  barricade, post a sign at that end of the stairway to warn employees
  of the hazard.

<!-- -->

- **[[3.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), a fixed
  ladder that is more than 6 m in length shall, where practicable, be
  fitted with a protective cage for that portion of its length that is
  more than 2 m above the base level of the ladder.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), a fixed ladder that is
  more than 9 m in length shall have, at intervals of not more than 6 m,
  a landing or platform that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is not less than 0.36 m^2^ in area; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is fitted at its outer edges with a guardrail.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A fixed ladder, cage, landing or platform referred to
  in subsection (1) or (2) shall be designed and constructed to
  withstand all loads that may be imposed on it.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A fixed ladder shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} vertical;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} securely held in place at the top, bottom and at
    intermediate points; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} fitted with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} rungs that are at least 150 mm from the wall and
      uniformly spaced at intervals not exceeding 300 mm, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} side rails that extend not less than 900 mm
      above the landing or platform.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) do not apply to a fixed
  ladder that is used with a fall protection system referred to in
  section 13.10 of Part XIII.

### [Docks, Ramps and Dock Plates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908184 .Subheading}

- **[[3.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every loading and unloading dock and
  ramp shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} of sufficient strength to support the maximum load
    that is likely to be imposed on it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} free of surface irregularities that may interfere
    with the safe operation of mobile equipment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} fitted around its sides that are not used for
    loading or unloading with side rails, curbs or rolled edges of
    sufficient height and strength to prevent mobile equipment from
    running over the edge.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every portable ramp and every dock plate shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} clearly marked or tagged to indicate the maximum
    safe load that it is capable of supporting; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} installed so that it cannot slide, move or
    otherwise be displaced under the load that may be imposed on it.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 9(E)
:::

### [Guardrails]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908195 .Subheading}

- **[[3.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every guardrail shall consist of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a horizontal top rail or line not less than 900 mm
    and not more than 1 100 mm above the base of the guardrail;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a horizontal intermediate rail or line spaced
    midway between the top rail or line and the base of the guardrail;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} supporting posts spaced not more than 3 m apart at
    their centres.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every guardrail shall be designed to withstand the
  greater of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the maximum load that is likely to be imposed on
    it; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a static load of not less than 890 N applied in any
    direction at any point on the top rail or line.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 10(E)
:::

**[[3.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.10 .sectionLabel}** Where it is not
practicable to install guardrails as required by subsection 3.3(5) or
subsection 4.8(1) or paragraph 4.11(2)(c) of Part IV, cables or chains
shall be installed in a manner that will prevent employees from falling
from the work place.

### [Toe Boards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908207 .Subheading}

- **[[3.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), if there
  is a hazard that tools or other objects may fall from a platform or
  other raised area onto an employee, the employer shall, to the extent
  possible, install

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a toe board that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} extends above the floor of the raised area, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} prevents tools or other objects from falling; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the tools or the other objects are piled to such
    a height that a toe board will not prevent the tools or the other
    objects from falling, a solid or mesh panel that extends from the
    floor of the raised area to a height of not less than 450 mm.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If it is not possible to install a toe board on a
  platform or other raised area, all tools or other objects that could
  fall shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} tied in a manner that, if they fall, employees
    beneath the platform will be protected; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} placed in a way that, if they fall, they will be
    caught by a safety net that shall be positioned so as to protect
    from injury any employee on or below the platform or other raised
    area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 11
- SOR/2014-141, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-3.11-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 3.11"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Housekeeping and Maintenance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908219 .Subheading}

- **[[3.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every stairway, walkway, ramp and
  passageway used by employees shall, as far as is practicable, be kept
  free of accumulations of ice and snow.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} All dust, dirt, waste and scrap material in a work
  place shall be removed as often as is necessary to protect the safety
  and health of employees and shall be disposed of in such a manner that
  the safety and health of employees is not compromised.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every travelled surface in a work place shall be
  maintained free from splinters, holes, loose boards and tiles or
  similar defects.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 12(F)
:::

- **[[3.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a floor in a work place is
  normally wet and employees in the work place do not use non-slip
  footwear, the floor shall be covered with a dry false floor or
  platform or treated with a non-slip material or substance.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The floor in a work place shall, as far as is
  practicable, be kept free from oil, grease or any other slippery
  substance.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 13
:::

### [Temporary Heat]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908231 .Subheading}

- **[[3.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), where a
  salamander or other portable open-flame heating device is used in an
  enclosed work place, the heating device shall not restrict a means of
  exit and shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} so located, protected and used that there is no
    hazard of igniting combustible materials adjacent to the heating
    device;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} used only when there is ventilation provided that
    protects the safety and health of employees; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} so located as to be protected from damage or
    overturning.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the heating device referred to in
  subsection (1) does not provide complete combustion of the fuel used
  in connection with it, the heating device shall be equipped with a
  securely supported sheet metal pipe that discharges the products of
  combustion outside the enclosed work place.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A portable fire extinguisher that has not less than a
  10B rating as defined in the ULC Standard shall be readily accessible
  from the location of the heating device referred to in subsection (1)
  when the device is in use.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 14
:::

## [PART IV]{.HLabel1}[Temporary Structures and Excavations]{.HTitleText1} {#h-908241 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908242 .Subheading}

**[[4.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[stage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a working platform supported from
above. ([*plate-forme suspendue*]{.otherLang lang="fr"})

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908244 .Subheading}

**[[4.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.2 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies to
fixed and portable ladders, to stages and scaffolds and to temporary
ramps and stairs.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 15(E)
:::

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908248 .Subheading}

**[[4.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.3 .sectionLabel}** No employee shall work
on a temporary structure in environmental conditions that are likely to
be hazardous to the safety or health of the employee, except where the
work is required to remove a hazard or to rescue an employee.

**[[4.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.4 .sectionLabel}** Tools, equipment and
materials used on a temporary structure shall be arranged or secured in
such a manner that they cannot be knocked off the structure
accidentally.

**[[4.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.5 .sectionLabel}** No employee shall use a
temporary structure unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} he has authority from his employer to use it; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} he has been trained and instructed in its safe and
  proper use.

<!-- -->

- **[[4.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before a temporary structure is used
  by an employee, a qualified person shall make a visual safety
  inspection of it.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an inspection made in accordance with
  subsection (1) reveals a defect or condition that adversely affects
  the structural integrity of a temporary structure, no employee shall
  use the temporary structure until the defect or condition is remedied.

### [Barricades]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908257 .Subheading}

**[[4.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.7 .sectionLabel}** Where a vehicle or a
pedestrian may come into contact with a temporary structure, a person
shall be positioned at the base of the temporary structure or a
barricade shall be installed around it to prevent any such contact.

### [Guardrails and Toe Boards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908259 .Subheading}

- **[[4.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 3.10 of Part III,
  at every open edge of a platform of a temporary structure guardrails
  shall be installed and, subject to subsection 3.11(2) of Part III,
  where there is a likelihood that persons beneath the platform may be
  injured by objects falling from the platform, toe boards shall be
  installed.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The guardrails and toe boards referred to in
  subsection (1) shall meet the standards set out in section 3.9 and
  subsection 3.11(1) of Part III.

### [Temporary Stairs, Ramps and Platforms]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908263 .Subheading}

- **[[4.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 4.10(3),
  temporary stairs, ramps and platforms shall be designed, constructed
  and maintained to support any load that is likely to be imposed on
  them and to allow safe passage of persons and equipment on them.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Temporary stairs shall have

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} uniform steps in the same flight;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a slope not exceeding 1.2 in 1; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a hand-rail that is not less than 900 mm and not
    more than 1 100 mm above the stair level on open sides including
    landings.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Temporary ramps and platforms shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} securely fastened in place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} braced if necessary to ensure their stability; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} provided with cleats or surfaced in a manner that
    provides a safe footing for employees.

### [Scaffolds]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908274 .Subheading}

- **[[4.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.10
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The erection, use, dismantling or
  removal of a scaffold shall be carried out by or under the supervision
  of a qualified person.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a scaffold is erected on an uneven surface, it
  shall be provided with base plates that maintain its stability.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every scaffold shall be capable of supporting at
  least four times the load that is likely to be imposed on it.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every scaffold shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have a platform that is at least 500 mm wide and
    securely fastened in place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have a working surface that is even and horizontal;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be fitted with guardrails except on the side where
    the work to be performed would be hindered by the guardrail.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The footings and supports of every scaffold shall be
  capable of carrying, without dangerous settling, all loads that are
  likely to be imposed on them.

### [Stages]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908284 .Subheading}

- **[[4.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The erection, use, dismantling or
  removal of a stage shall be carried out by or under the supervision of
  a qualified person.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every stage shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have a working surface that is even and horizontal
    and is capable of supporting any load that is likely to be imposed
    on it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be fitted with an effective means of holding the
    stage away from the working area; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to section 3.10 of Part III, where the
    stage is to be used at a height of more than 3 m, be fitted with
    guardrails.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The supporting structure and the ropes or tackle
  supporting a stage shall have a safety factor of not less than six.

### [Ladders]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908292 .Subheading}

- **[[4.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Commercially manufactured portable
  ladders shall meet the standards set out in CSA Standard CAN3-Z11-M81,
  Portable Ladders, the English version of which is dated September
  1981, as amended to March 1983, and the French version of which is
  dated August 1982, as amended to June 1983.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), every fixed and portable
  ladder shall, while being used,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be placed on a firm footing; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be secured in such a manner that it cannot be
    dislodged accidentally from its position.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every fixed or portable ladder shall be positioned in
  such a manner that it is not necessary for a person to use the
  underside of the ladder.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a fixed or portable ladder provides access from
  one level to another

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the ladder shall extend, where practicable, at
    least three rungs above the higher level; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where it is not practicable to comply with
    paragraph (a), handholds shall be provided.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} No metal or wire-bound fixed or portable ladder shall
  be used where there is a hazard that it may come into contact with any
  live electrical circuit or equipment.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} No employee shall work from any of the three top
  rungs of any single or extension portable ladder or from either of the
  two top steps of any step ladder.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} No non-metallic fixed or portable ladder shall be
  coated with a material that may hide flaws.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344480){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344480.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-4.12-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 4.12"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Excavation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908305 .Subheading}

- **[[4.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before the commencement of work on
  an excavation, trench or tunnel or the creation of an opening in a
  bulkhead, deck or similar structure, the employer shall mark the
  location of all pipes, cables and conduits in the area where the work
  is to be done.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an excavation, trench or opening constitutes a
  hazard to employees, a barricade shall be installed around it.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an employee is required to enter an excavation
  or trench that is more than 1.4 m deep and the sides of which are
  sloped at an angle of 45° or more to the horizontal, or a tunnel,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the walls of the excavation, trench or tunnel, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the roof of the tunnel

  shall be supported by shoring and bracing that is installed as the
  excavation, trench or tunnel is being excavated.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (3) does not apply in respect of a trench
  where the employer provides a system of shoring composed of steel
  plates and bracing, welded or bolted together, that can support the
  walls of the trench from the ground level to the trench bottom and can
  be moved along as work progresses.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The installation and removal of the shoring and
  bracing referred to in subsection (3) shall be performed or supervised
  by a qualified person.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Tools, machinery, timber, excavated materials or
  other objects shall not be placed within 1 m from the edge of an
  excavation, trench or opening.

### [Safety Nets]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908316 .Subheading}

- **[[4.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where there is a hazard that tools,
  equipment or materials may fall onto or from a temporary structure,
  the employer shall provide a protective structure or a safety net to
  protect from injury any employee on or below the temporary structure.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The design, construction and installation of a safety
  net referred to in subsection (1) shall meet the standards set out in
  ANSI Standard ANSI A10.11-1979, American National Standard for Safety
  Nets Used During Construction, Repair and Demolition Operations, dated
  August 7, 1979.

### [Housekeeping]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908320 .Subheading}

**[[4.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.15 .sectionLabel}** Every platform,
hand-rail, guardrail and work area on a temporary structure used by an
employee shall, as far as is practicable, be kept free of accumulations
of ice and snow while the temporary structure is in use.

**[[4.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.16 .sectionLabel}** The working surface
of a temporary structure used by an employee shall, where practicable,
be kept free of grease, oil or other slippery substance and of any
material or object that may cause an employee to slip or trip.

## [PART V]{.HLabel1}[Elevating Devices]{.HTitleText1} {#h-908323 .Part}

### [Standards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908324 .Subheading}

- **[[5.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every elevating device and every
  safety device attached thereto shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} meet the standards set out in the applicable CSA
    standard referred to in subsection (2), so far as is reasonably
    practicable; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be used, operated and maintained in accordance with
    the standards set out in the applicable CSA standard referred to in
    subsection (2).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the applicable
  CSA standard for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} elevators, dumbwaiters, escalators and moving walks
    is CSA Standard CAN/CSA --- --- B44-M90, Safety Code for Elevators,
    the English version of which is dated May 1990 and the French
    version of which is dated December 1990, other than clause 9.1.4;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} manlifts is CSA Standard B311-M1979, Safety Code
    for Manlifts, the English version of which is dated October 1979 and
    the French version of which is dated July 1984, and Supplement No.
    1-1984 to B311-M1979, the English version of which is dated June
    1984 and the French version of which is dated August 1984; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} elevating devices for the handicapped is CSA
    Standard CAN3-B355-M81, Safety Code for Elevating Devices for the
    Handicapped, the English version of which is dated April 1981 and
    the French version of which is dated December 1981.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 16
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344481){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344481.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5.1-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 5.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Personnel Transfer Baskets]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908335 .Subheading}

- **[[5.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  basket shall be used to transfer freight except in an emergency.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every transfer of a person by a basket shall be made
  only when visibility and environmental conditions are such that the
  transfer can be made safely.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a person is transferred by a basket to or from
  a place on a ship or to or from a place on a drilling unit or an
  offshore production facility,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} persons at both places shall be in direct radio
    contact; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person to be transferred shall

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} be instructed in the safety procedures to be
      followed by him, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} shall use a life jacket or a personal flotation
      device.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a person is transferred by a basket to or from
  a drilling unit or an offshore production facility, the drilling unit
  or production facility shall be equipped with at least two buoyant
  baskets.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every basket shall be in serviceable condition and
  all ropes, wires or other vital parts of a basket that show signs of
  significant wear shall be replaced before the basket is used.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} The number of persons transferred in a basket shall
  not exceed the number of persons the basket was designed to carry
  safely.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} The raising or lowering of a basket shall, as far as
  is practicable, be carried out over water.

### [Use and Operation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908348 .Subheading}

**[[5.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.3 .sectionLabel}** No elevating device
shall be used or operated

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} with a load in excess of the load that it was
  designed and installed to move safely; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the elevating device is installed on a floating
  drilling unit or a floating production facility, when the roll of the
  drilling unit or the production facility exceeds the maximum roll
  recommended by the manufacturer for the safe operation of the
  elevating device.

<!-- -->

- **[[5.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), no
  elevating device shall be used or placed in service while any safety
  device attached thereto is inoperative.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), no safety device attached
  to an elevating device shall be altered, interfered with or rendered
  inoperative.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) do not apply to an elevating
  device or a safety device that is being inspected, tested, repaired or
  maintained by a qualified person.

### [Inspection and Testing]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908356 .Subheading}

**[[5.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.5 .sectionLabel}** Every elevating device
and every safety device attached thereto shall be inspected and tested
by a qualified person to determine that the prescribed standards are met

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} before the elevating device or the safety device
  attached thereto is placed in service;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} after an alteration to the elevating device or a
  safety device attached thereto; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} once every 12 months.

<!-- -->

- **[[5.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  record of each inspection and test made in accordance with section 5.5
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be signed by the qualified person who made the
    inspection and test;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} include the date of the inspection and test and the
    identification and location of the elevating device and safety
    device that were inspected and tested; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the observations of the qualified person
    inspecting and testing the elevating device and safety device on the
    safety of the devices.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every record referred to in subsection (1) shall be
  kept by the employer for five years after the date on which it is
  signed.

### [Repair and Maintenance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908367 .Subheading}

**[[5.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.7 .sectionLabel}** Repair and maintenance
of elevating devices and safety devices attached thereto shall be
performed by a qualified person appointed by the employer.

## [PART VI]{.HLabel1}[Boilers and Pressure Vessels]{.HTitleText1} {#h-908369 .Part}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2014-141, s. 15(F)

\]
:::

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908372 .Subheading}

**[[6.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[inspector]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[inspector]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a qualified person
    recognized under the laws of Canada or of a province as qualified to
    inspect boilers, pressure vessels or piping systems;
    ([inspecteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[maximum allowable working pressure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[maximum allowable working pressure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
    maximum allowable working pressure set out in the record referred to
    in section 6.12; ([pression de fonctionnement maximale
    autorisée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[maximum temperature]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[maximum temperature]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the maximum
    temperature set out in the record referred to in section 6.12;
    ([température maximale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[piping system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[piping system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an assembly of pipes,
    pipe fittings, valves, safety devices, pumps, compressors and other
    fixed equipment that contains a gas, vapour or liquid and is
    connected to a boiler or pressure vessel. ([réseau de
    canalisation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 17(F)
- SOR/2014-141, s. 15(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.1-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 6.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908380 .Subheading}

**[[6.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.2 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a heating boiler that has a heating surface of 3 m^2^
  or less;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that has a capacity of 40 L or
  less;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that is installed for use at a
  pressure of 100 kPa or less;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that has an internal diameter of
  150 mm or less;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that has an internal diameter of
  600 mm or less and that is used for the storage of hot water;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that has an internal diameter of
  600 mm or less and that is connected to a water-pumping system
  containing air that is compressed to serve as a cushion; or

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} a refrigeration plant that has a capacity of 18 kW or
  less of refrigeration.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-141, s. 15(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.2-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 6.2"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Construction, Testing and Installation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908391 .Subheading}

**[[6.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.3 .sectionLabel}** Every boiler, pressure
vessel and piping system used in a work place shall be constructed,
tested and installed by a qualified person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-141, s. 15(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 6.3"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Use, Operation, Repair, Alteration and Maintenance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908395 .Subheading}

- **[[6.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall use a boiler, pressure vessel or piping system unless it
  has been inspected by an inspector in accordance with subsection (2).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every boiler, pressure vessel and piping system shall
  be inspected in accordance with sections 6.8 to 6.10

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} after installation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after any welding, alteration or repair is carried
    out on it.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 18
- SOR/2014-141, s. 15(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.4-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 6.4"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[6.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.5 .sectionLabel}** Every boiler, pressure
vessel and piping system in use at a work place shall be operated,
maintained and repaired by a qualified person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-141, s. 15(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.5-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 6.5"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[6.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.6 .sectionLabel}** No person shall alter,
interfere with or render inoperative any fitting attached to a boiler,
pressure vessel or piping system except for the purpose of adjusting or
testing the fitting.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-141, s. 15(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.6-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 6.6"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Buried Pressure Vessels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908409 .Subheading}

**[[6.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.7 .sectionLabel}** Notice of a proposed
backfilling over a buried pressure vessel shall be given to the Head of
Compliance and Enforcement before the backfilling is begun.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-141, s. 2(F)
- SOR/2014-148, s. 23
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 9](#1295161-1312177){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295161-1312177.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.7-20141031.html "Link to previous version of section 6.7"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Inspections]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908413 .Subheading}

- **[[6.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 6.9, every
  boiler, pressure vessel and piping system in use in a work place shall
  be inspected

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} externally, at least once each year; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} internally, at least once every five years.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(a) does not apply to a pressure vessel
  that is buried.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-141, s. 15(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.8-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 6.8"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[6.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a pressure vessel is used to
  store anhydrous ammonia, a hydrostatic test at a pressure equal to one
  and one-half times the maximum allowable working pressure shall be
  conducted at least once every five years.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The integrity of a pressure vessel that is a part of
  a motion compensator system or blowout preventer shall be verified at
  least once every five years by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where practicable, an internal inspection; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where an internal inspection is not practicable, by
    a hydrostatic test or other non-destructive test method.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-141, s. 15(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.9-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 6.9"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[6.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.10
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where more than five years have
  elapsed since the date of the last test and inspection of a Halon
  container, the container shall not be recharged without a test of
  container strength and a complete visual inspection being carried out.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Halon container that has been continuously in
  service without being discharged may be retained in service for a
  maximum of 20 years after the date of the last test and inspection, at
  which time it shall be emptied, subjected to a test of container
  strength and a complete visual inspection and re-marked before being
  placed back in service.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a Halon container has been subjected to unusual
  corrosion, shock or vibration, a complete visual inspection and a test
  of container strength shall be carried out.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 19
:::

**[[6.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.11 .sectionLabel}** In addition to the
requirements of sections 6.8 to 6.10, every boiler, pressure vessel and
piping system in use at a work place shall be inspected by a qualified
person as frequently as is necessary to ensure that the boiler, pressure
vessel or piping system is safe for its intended use.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-141, s. 15(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.11-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 6.11"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908437 .Subheading}

- **[[6.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.12 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  record of each inspection carried out under sections 6.4 and 6.8 to
  6.11 shall be completed by the inspector or qualified person who
  carried out the inspection.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every record referred to in subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall be signed by the inspector or qualified
    person who carried out the inspection; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall include

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date of the inspection,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the identification and location of the boiler,
      pressure vessel or piping system that was inspected,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the maximum allowable working pressure and the
      maximum temperature at which the boiler or pressure vessel may be
      operated,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a declaration as to whether the boiler, pressure
      vessel or piping system meets the standards prescribed by this
      Part,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a declaration as to whether, in the opinion of
      the inspector or qualified person who carried out the inspection,
      the boiler, pressure vessel or piping system is safe for its
      intended use,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} where appropriate in the opinion of the
      inspector or qualified person who carried out the inspection,
      recommendations regarding the need for more frequent inspections
      or tests than are required by section 6.8, 6.9 or 6.10, and

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} any other observation that the inspector or
      qualified person who carried out considers relevant to the safety
      of employees.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall keep every record referred to in
  subsection (1) for one year after the date that the next inspection is
  required by this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-141, s. 15(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.12-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 6.12"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART VII]{.HLabel1}[Levels of Lighting]{.HTitleText1} {#h-908453 .Part}

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908454 .Subheading}

**[[7.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.1 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply to the bridge of a drilling unit or floating production facility.

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908456 .Subheading}

- **[[7.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  levels of lighting prescribed in this Part shall, where reasonably
  practicable, be provided by a lighting system installed by the
  employer.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where it is not reasonably practicable to comply with
  subsection (1), the employer shall provide portable lighting that
  gives the prescribed levels of lighting.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 20(F)
:::

### [Measurement of Average Levels of Lighting]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908462 .Subheading}

**[[7.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.3 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Part, the average level of lighting at a work position or in an
area shall be determined by making four or more measurements at
different places at the work position or in the area

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where work is performed at a level higher than the
  floor, at the level at which the work is performed, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, 1 m above the floor,

and dividing the aggregate of the results of the measurements by the
number of the measurements.

### [Minimum Average Levels of Lighting]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908467 .Subheading}

**[[7.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.4 .sectionLabel}** The average level of
lighting at a work position or in an area referred to in Column I of an
item of the Schedule to this Part shall be not less than the average
level set out in Column II of that item.

### [Emergency Lighting Systems]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908469 .Subheading}

- **[[7.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a failure in the lighting
  system in an area through which an employee passes in carrying out
  emergency procedures referred to in subsection 18.9(1) will cause the
  level of lighting to be reduced to less than 3 dalx, an emergency
  lighting system shall be installed in the area.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The emergency lighting system referred to in
  subsection (1) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} operate automatically in the event of a failure of
    the lighting system; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide an average level of lighting of 3 dalx.

### [Minimum Levels of Lighting]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908475 .Subheading}

**[[7.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.6 .sectionLabel}** The level of lighting
at any place at a work position or in an area shall be not less than
one-third of the average level of lighting prescribed by this Part for
the work position or area.

:::::: section
::::: {#908477 .Schedule}
::: {}

</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom"

<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> Work positions at which first
aid is rendered or examinations are conducted or at which tasks
essential to the safety or health of an employee are performed<img
src="/img/ii_spacer.gif" class="LeaderRightJustified-dot"
alt="blank line" /></p></li>
</ul></td>
<td class="noBorderBottom bottom" headers="t83h2 t83h4">80</td>

</ul></td>
<td class="noBorderBottom bottom" headers="t83h2 t83h4">20</td>
</tr>
<tr>

## [PART VIII]{.HLabel1}[Levels of Sound]{.HTitleText1} {#h-908487 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908488 .Subheading}

**[[8.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part, [[sound
level meter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an instrument for measuring
levels of sound and impulse sound that meets the standards set out in
ANSI Standard ANSI SI.4-1983, Specification for Sound Level Meters,
dated February 17, 1983, and is referred to in that Standard as type 0,
1 or 2. ([*sonomètre*]{.otherLang lang="fr"})

### [Levels of Sound]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908490 .Subheading}

- **[[8.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3)
  and sections 8.3 and 8.4, the level of sound in a work place shall be
  less than 85 dB.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where it is not reasonably practicable for an
  employer to maintain the level of sound in a work place at less than
  85 dB, no employee shall be exposed in any 24 hour period to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a level of sound referred to in Column I of an item
    of the schedule to this Part for a number of hours exceeding the
    number set out in Column II of that item; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a number of different levels of sound referred to
    in Column I of an item of the schedule to this Part, where the sum
    of the following quotients exceeds 1:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the number of hours of exposure to each level of
      sound

    divided by

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the maximum number of hours of exposure per
      24-hour period set out in Column II of that item.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where it is not reasonably practicable for an
  employer to maintain the exposure of an employee to a level of sound
  at or below the levels referred to in subsection (1) or (2), the
  employer shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} make a report in writing to the Head of Compliance
    and Enforcement setting out the reasons why the exposure cannot be
    so maintained; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide every employee entering the work place with
    a hearing protector that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} meets the standards set out in CSA Standard
      Z94.2-M1984, Hearing Protectors, the English version of which is
      dated June 1984 and the French version of which is dated February
      1985, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} reduces the level of sound reaching the
      employee's ears to less than 85 dB.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 21
- SOR/2014-148, s. 24
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 9](#1295161-1312179){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295161-1312179.html"}
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344482){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344482.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.2-20210701.html "Link to previous version of section 8.2"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[8.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.3 .sectionLabel}** No employee shall be
exposed in sleeping quarters to a level of sound of more than 75 dB.

**[[8.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.4 .sectionLabel}** Where the level of
impulse sound in a work place exceeds 140 dB, the employer shall provide
every employee entering the work place with a hearing protector that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} meets the standards set out in CSA Standard
  Z94.2-M1984, Hearing Protectors, the English version of which is dated
  June 1984 and the French version of which is dated February 1985; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} reduces the peak level of impulse sound reaching the
  employee's ears to 140 dB or less.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344484){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344484.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8.4-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8.4"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Sound Level Measurement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908510 .Subheading}

**[[8.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.5 .sectionLabel}** The levels of sound
referred to in sections 8.2 and 8.3 shall be measured by using the slow
exponential-time-averaging characteristic and the A-weighting
characteristic of a sound level meter.

**[[8.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.6 .sectionLabel}** The level of impulse
sound referred to in section 8.4 shall be measured by using the impulse
exponential-time-averaging characteristic of a sound level meter.

### [Warning Signs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908513 .Subheading}

**[[8.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8.7 .sectionLabel}** In a work place where
the level of sound is 85 dB or more or where the peak level of impulse
sound exceeds 140 dB, the employer shall post signs warning persons
entering the work place

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} that there is a hazardous level of sound or impulse
  sound in the work place;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, of the maximum number of hours of
  exposure determined under subsection 8.2(2); and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, of the requirement to wear a hearing
  protector.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 22(F)
:::

## [PART IX]{.HLabel1}[Electrical Safety]{.HTitleText1} {#h-908528 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908529 .Subheading}

**[[9.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,[[control
device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a device that will safely disconnect
electrical equipment from its source of energy. ([*dispositif de
commande*]{.otherLang lang="fr"})

### [Safety Procedures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908531 .Subheading}

- **[[9.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  testing or work performed on electrical equipment shall be performed
  by a qualified person or an employee under the direct supervision of a
  qualified person.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where there is a likelihood that the qualified person
  or the employee referred to in subsection (1) may receive a hazardous
  electrical shock during the performance of testing or work,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the qualified person or the employee shall use such
    insulated protection equipment and tools as will protect him from
    injury during the performance of the work; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee shall be instructed and trained in the
    use of the insulated protection equipment and tools.

<!-- -->

- **[[9.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where electrical equipment is live
  or may become live, no employee shall work on the equipment unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employer has instructed the employee in
    procedures that are safe for work on live conductors;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a safety ground is connected to the equipment; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the equipment is isolated in accordance with
    section 9.10.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and (4), where an employee
  is working on or near electrical equipment that is live or may become
  live, the electrical equipment shall be guarded.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), where it is not
  practicable for electrical equipment referred to in subsection (2) to
  be guarded, the employer shall take measures to protect the employee
  from injury by insulating the equipment from the employee or the
  employee from ground.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where live electrical equipment is not guarded or
  insulated in accordance with subsection (2) or (3) or where the
  employee referred to in subsection (3) is not insulated from ground,
  no employee shall work so near to any live part of the electrical
  equipment that is within a voltage range set out in Column I of an
  item of the schedule to this Part that the distance between the body
  of the employee or any thing with which the employee is in contact and
  the live part of the equipment is less than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the distance set out in Column II of that item,
    where the employee is not a qualified person; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the distance set out in Column III of that item,
    where the employee is a qualified person.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} No employee shall work near a live part of any
  electrical equipment referred to in subsection (4) where there is a
  hazard that an unintentional movement by the employee would bring any
  part of his body or any thing with which he is in contact closer to
  that live part than the distance referred to in that subsection.

**[[9.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.4 .sectionLabel}** No employee shall work
on or near high voltage electrical equipment unless he is authorized to
do so by his employer.

**[[9.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.5 .sectionLabel}** A legible sign with the
words "DANGER --- HIGH VOLTAGE" and "DANGER --- HAUTE TENSION" in
letters that are not less than 50 mm in height on a contrasting
background or a symbol conveying the same meaning shall be posted in a
conspicuous place at every approach to live high voltage electrical
equipment.

### [Safety Watcher]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908550 .Subheading}

- **[[9.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an employee is working on or
  near live electrical equipment and, because of the nature of the work
  or the condition or location of the work place, it is necessary for
  the safety of the employee that the work be observed by a person not
  engaged in the work, the employer shall appoint a safety watcher

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to warn all employees in the work place of the
    hazard; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to ensure that all safety precautions and
    procedures are complied with.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A safety watcher shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} informed of his duties as a safety watcher and of
    the hazard involved in the work;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} trained and instructed in the procedures to follow
    in the event of an emergency;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorized to stop immediately any part of the work
    that he considers dangerous; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} free of any other duties that might interfere with
    his duties as a safety watcher.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), an employer may
  appoint himself as a safety watcher.

### [Coordination of Work]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908561 .Subheading}

**[[9.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.7 .sectionLabel}** Where an employee is
working on or in connection with electrical equipment, the employee and
every other person who is so working, including every safety watcher,
shall be fully informed by the employer with respect to the safe
coordination of their work.

### [Poles and Elevated Structures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908563 .Subheading}

- **[[9.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before an employee climbs a pole or
  elevated structure that is embedded in the ground and is used to
  support electrical equipment, the employer shall give instructions and
  training to the employee respecting inspections and tests of the pole
  or structure to be carried out before the pole or structure is
  climbed.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, as a result of an inspection or test of a pole
  or elevated structure referred to in subsection (1), it appears to an
  employee that the pole or structure will be safe for climbing only
  when temporary supports have been installed, pike-poles alone shall
  not be used for the supports.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 23(F)
:::

**[[9.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.9 .sectionLabel}** Every pole or elevated
structure referred to in subsection 9.8(1) shall meet the standards set
out in

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard CAN3-015-M83, Wood Utility Poles and
  Reinforcing Studs, the English version of which is dated January 1983
  and the French version of which is dated December 1983; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard A14-M1979, Concrete Poles, the English
  version of which is dated September 1979 and the French version of
  which is dated November 1987.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 24
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344485){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344485.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9.9-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 9.9"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Isolation of Electrical Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908574 .Subheading}

- **[[9.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.10
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before an employee isolates
  electrical equipment or changes or terminates the isolation of
  electrical equipment, the employer shall issue written instructions
  with respect to the procedures to be followed for the safe performance
  of that work.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The instructions referred to in subsection (1) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} state the isolation procedures to be followed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify the electrical equipment to which the
    instructions apply;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} describe any tests to be performed;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} specify particulars of the tags or signs to be
    used; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} specify the protection equipment to be used.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A tag or sign referred to in paragraph (2)(d) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} contain the words "DO NOT OPERATE --- DÉFENSE
    D'ACTIONNER" or display a symbol conveying the same meaning;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} show the date and hour at which the electrical
    equipment was isolated;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} show the name of the employee performing the work
    or live test;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where used in connection with a live test, be
    distinctively marked as a testing tag or sign;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} be removed only by the employee performing the work
    or live test; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} be used for no purpose other than to notify persons
    that the operation or movement of the electrical equipment is
    prohibited during the performance of the work or live test.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the instructions referred to in
  subsection (1) shall be shown and explained to the employee.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The instructions referred to in subsection (1) shall
  be kept readily available for examination by employees at the work
  place in which the electrical equipment is located.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 25
:::

### [Control Devices, Switches, Cords and Cables]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908594 .Subheading}

- **[[9.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every control device shall be so
  designed and located as to permit quick and safe operation at all
  times.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The path of access to every electrical switch,
  control device or meter shall be free from obstruction.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an electrical switch or other control device
  controlling the supply of electrical energy to electrical equipment is
  operated only by a person authorized to do so by the employer, the
  switch or other control device shall be fitted with a locking device
  that only such an authorized person can activate.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Control switches for all electrically operated
  machinery shall be clearly marked to indicate the switch positions
  that correspond to the electrical circuits being controlled.

<!-- -->

- **[[9.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All electrical equipment within a
  Class 1 Division 1 area or Class 1 Division 2 area as defined in the
  Canadian Electrical Code shall be constructed in a manner suitable for
  the environmental conditions in the area in which the equipment is
  used.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each extension cord of electrical equipment referred
  to in subsection (1) shall be equipped with a terminal that provides
  an interruption of the circuit before a connecting device is
  withdrawn.

### [Defective Electrical Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908603 .Subheading}

**[[9.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.13 .sectionLabel}** Defective electrical
equipment that is likely to be hazardous to the safety or health of an
employee shall be disconnected from its power source by a means other
than the control switch and notices shall be placed on the equipment and
at the control switch to indicate that the equipment is defective.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 26(F)
:::

### [Electrical Fuses]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908607 .Subheading}

- **[[9.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Electrical fuses shall be of the
  correct ampere rating and fault capacity rating for the circuit in
  which they are installed.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No employee shall replace missing or burnt-out fuses
  unless authorized to so do by a qualified person.

### [Power Supply Cables]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908611 .Subheading}

- **[[9.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Power supply cables for portable
  electrical equipment shall be placed clear of areas used for vehicles
  unless the cables are protected by guards.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A three-wire power supply cable on electrical
  equipment or on an electrical appliance shall not be altered or
  changed for the purpose of using the equipment or appliance on a
  two-wire power supply.

### [Grounded Electrical Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-908615 .Subheading}

**[[9.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.16 .sectionLabel}** Grounded electrical
equipment and appliances shall be used only when connected to a matching
electrical outlet receptacle.

::::: section
:::: {#908617 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [SCHEDULE]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subsection 9.3(4))]{.OriginatingRef} {#h-908618 .scheduleLabel}
