  possession of a supplier safety data sheet that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is for a hazardous product that both has the same
    product identifier and is from the same supplier;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} discloses information that is current at the time
    that the hazardous product is received; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} was prepared and dated less than three years before
    the day on which the hazardous product is received.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the supplier safety data sheet in respect of a
  hazardous product in a work place is three years old or more, the
  employer shall, if possible, obtain from the supplier a current
  supplier safety data sheet.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If it is not possible for an employer to obtain a
  current supplier safety data sheet, the employer shall update the
  hazard information on the most recent supplier safety data sheet that
  the employer has received, on the basis of the ingredients disclosed
  on that supplier safety data sheet and on the basis of any significant
  new data of which the employer is aware.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer is exempt from the requirements of
  subsection (1) if a laboratory sample of a hazardous product is
  received in a work place from a supplier who is exempted by the
  [Hazardous Products Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17) from
  the requirement to provide a safety data sheet for that product.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
- SOR/2016-141, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.34-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 11.34"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Work Place Safety Data Sheets]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909180 .Subheading}

- **[[11.35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.35
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 11.44, if an
  employer produces in the work place a hazardous product, other than a
  fugitive emission or an intermediate product undergoing reaction
  within a reaction or process vessel, or imports into Canada a
  hazardous product and brings it into the work place, the employer
  shall prepare a work place safety data sheet in respect of that
  hazardous product.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 11.44, if an employer receives a
  supplier safety data sheet, the employer may prepare a work place
  safety data sheet to be used in the work place in place of the
  supplier safety data sheet if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the work place safety data sheet discloses at least
    the information disclosed on the supplier safety data sheet;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the information disclosed on the work place safety
    data sheet does not disclaim or contradict the information disclosed
    on the supplier safety data sheet;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the supplier safety data sheet is readily available
    for examination by employees in any form, as determined in
    consultation with the policy committee or, if there is no policy
    committee, the safety and health committee or the safety and health
    representative; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the work place safety data sheet discloses that the
    supplier safety data sheet is available in the work place.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall review the accuracy of the
  information disclosed on a work place safety data sheet referred to in
  subsection (1) or (2) and update it as soon as practicable after new
  hazard information or significant new data becomes available to the
  employer.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the information required to be disclosed on the
  work place safety data sheet is not available or not applicable to the
  hazardous product, the employer shall, in place of the information,
  insert the words "not available" or "not applicable", as the case may
  be, in the English version and the words "[non disponible]{.otherLang
  lang="fr"}" or "[sans objet]{.otherLang lang="fr"}", as the case may
  be, in the French version, of the work place safety data sheet.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
- SOR/94-165, s. 41
- SOR/2016-141, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.35-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 11.35"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Availability of Safety Data Sheets]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909192 .Subheading}

- **[[11.36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.36
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall keep readily
  available for examination by employees and by any policy committee,
  safety and health committee or safety and health representative in any
  work place in which an employee is likely to handle or be exposed to a
  hazardous product a copy, in English and in French, of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an employer referred to in
    subsection 11.35(1) or (2), the work place safety data sheet; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the supplier safety data sheet.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The work place safety data sheet and supplier safety
  data sheet shall be made available in any form, as determined in
  consultation with the policy committee or, if there is no policy
  committee, the safety and health committee or the safety and health
  representative.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
- SOR/94-165, s. 42
- SOR/2016-141, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.36-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 11.36"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Labels]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909200 .Subheading}

- **[[11.37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.37
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 11.39 to 11.41,
  each hazardous product, other than a hazardous product referred to in
  paragraph 11.33(c), in a work place and each container in which the
  hazardous product is contained in a work place shall, if the hazardous
  product or the container is received from a supplier,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case where the hazardous product is in a
    bulk shipment, be accompanied by a supplier label;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case where the employer has undertaken in
    writing to the supplier to apply a label to the inner container of
    the hazardous product, have applied

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to the outer container a supplier label, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} as soon as possible after the hazardous product
      is received from the supplier, to the inner container a supplier
      label; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, have applied to it a supplier
    label.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 11.39 to 11.41 and 11.44, if a
  hazardous product, other than a hazardous product referred to in
  paragraph 11.33(c), is received from a supplier and an employer places
  the hazardous product in the work place in a container other than the
  container in which it was received from the supplier, the employer
  shall apply to the container a supplier label or work place label that
  discloses the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the product identifier;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the hazard information in respect of the hazardous
    product; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a statement indicating that a safety data sheet for
    the hazardous product is available in the work place.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 11.43 and 11.44, no person shall
  remove, deface, modify or alter the supplier label applied to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a hazardous product that is in the work place; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the container of a hazardous product that is in the
    work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
- SOR/2016-141, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.37-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 11.37"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[11.38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.38
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 11.40 and 11.41,
  if an employer produces in the work place a hazardous product, other
  than a fugitive emission, or imports into Canada a hazardous product
  and brings it into the work place, and the hazardous product is not in
  a container, the employer shall disclose the following information on
  a work place label applied to the hazardous product or on a sign
  posted in a conspicuous place in the work place:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the product identifier;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the hazard information in respect of the hazardous
    product; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a statement indicating that a work place safety
    data sheet for the hazardous product is available in the work place.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 11.39 to 11.41, if an employer
  produces in the work place a hazardous product, other than a fugitive
  emission, or imports into Canada a hazardous product and brings it
  into the work place, and the employer places the hazardous product in
  a container, the employer shall apply to the container a work place
  label that discloses the information referred to in paragraphs (1)(a)
  to (c).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply in respect of a
  hazardous product that is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} intended for export; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} packaged in a container for sale in Canada, if the
    container is or is in the process of being appropriately labelled
    for that purpose.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
- SOR/94-165, s. 43
- SOR/2016-141, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.38-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 11.38"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Portable Containers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909228 .Subheading}

**[[11.39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.39 .sectionLabel}** Where an employer
stores a hazardous product in the work place in a container that has
applied to it a supplier label or a work place label, a portable
container filled from that container does not have to be labelled in
accordance with section 11.37 or 11.38 if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the hazardous product is required for immediate use;
  or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the following conditions apply in respect of the
  hazardous product:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} it is under the control of and used exclusively by
    the employee who filled the portable container,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} it is used only during the work shift in which the
    portable container was filled, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} it is clearly identified by a work place label
    applied to the portable container that discloses the product
    identifier.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
- SOR/2016-141, ss. 47(F), 53
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.39-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 11.39"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Special Cases]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909237 .Subheading}

**[[11.40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.40 .sectionLabel}** An employer shall,
in a conspicuous place near a hazardous product, post a sign in respect
of the hazardous product that discloses the product identifier if the
hazardous product is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in a process, reaction or storage vessel;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a continuous-run container;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a bulk shipment that is not placed in a container at
  the work place; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} not in a container and stored in bulk.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
- SOR/2016-141, s. 48
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.40-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 11.40"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Laboratories]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909245 .Subheading}

- **[[11.41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.41
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a laboratory sample of a
  hazardous product is the subject of a labelling exemption under
  subsection 5(5) of the [Hazardous Products
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17), a label that is provided
  by the supplier and is affixed to, printed on or attached to the
  container of the sample received at the work place and that discloses
  the following information in place of the information required under
  paragraph 3(1)(d) of those Regulations is considered to comply with
  the requirements set out in section 11.37 with respect to a supplier
  label:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if known by the supplier, the chemical name or
    generic chemical name of any material that is in the hazardous
    product and that is classified under the [Hazardous Products
    Act](/eng/acts/H-3) and the [Hazardous Products
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17) as a biohazardous
    infectious material; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the statement "Hazardous Laboratory Sample. For
    hazard information or in an emergency, call/[Échantillon pour
    laboratoire de produit dangereux. Pour obtenir des renseignements
    sur les dangers ou en cas d'urgence, composez]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}", followed by an emergency telephone number for the
    purpose of obtaining the information that must be provided on the
    safety data sheet of a hazardous product.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a laboratory sample of a hazardous product is the
  subject of a labelling exemption under subsection 5(6) of the
  [Hazardous Products Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17), a
  label that is provided by the supplier and is affixed to, printed on
  or attached to the container of the sample received at the work place
  and that discloses the following information in place of the
  information required under paragraph 3(1)(c) or (d) of those
  Regulations is considered to comply with the requirements set out in
  section 11.37 with respect to a supplier label:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if known by the supplier, the chemical name or
    generic chemical name of any material or substance that is in the
    hazardous product and that is referred to in subsection 3(2) of
    Schedule 1 to the [Hazardous Products
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the statement "Hazardous Laboratory Sample. For
    hazard information or in an emergency, call/[Échantillon pour
    laboratoire de produit dangereux. Pour obtenir des renseignements
    sur les dangers ou en cas d'urgence, composez]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}", followed by an emergency telephone number for the
    purpose of obtaining the information that must be provided on the
    safety data sheet of a hazardous product.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a hazardous product is in a container other than
  the container in which it was received from a supplier or is produced
  in the work place, the employer is exempt from the requirements set
  out in section 11.38 and subparagraph 11.39(b)(iii) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employer has complied with subsection (4);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} employee education and training is provided as
    required by these Regulations; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the hazardous product

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a laboratory sample,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is intended by the employer to be used solely
      for analysis, testing or evaluation in a laboratory, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} is clearly identified through any mode of
      identification visible to employees at the work place.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (3)(a), the employer
  shall ensure that the mode of identification used and the employee
  education and training provided enable the employees to readily
  identify and obtain either the information required on a safety data
  sheet or the information set out in subsections (1) and (2) with
  respect to the hazardous product or laboratory sample.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer is exempt from the requirements of
  section 11.37 if a laboratory sample of a hazardous product is
  received in a work place from a supplier who is exempted by the
  [Hazardous Products Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2015-17) from
  the requirement to provide a label for that product.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
- SOR/2016-141, s. 49
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.41-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 11.41"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Signs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909264 .Subheading}

**[[11.42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.42 .sectionLabel}** The information
disclosed on a sign referred to in subsection 11.38(1), section 11.40 or
paragraph 11.45(b) shall be of such a size that it is clearly legible to
the employees in the work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
:::

#### [Replacing Labels]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909268 .Subheading}

- **[[11.43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.43
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, in a work place, a label applied
  to a hazardous product or the container of a hazardous product becomes
  illegible or is removed from the hazardous product or the container,
  the employer shall replace the label with a work place label that
  discloses the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the product identifier;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the hazard information in respect of the hazardous
    product; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a statement indicating that a safety data sheet for
    the hazardous product is available in the work place.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall review the accuracy of the
  information on a work place label and update it as soon as practicable
  after new hazard information or significant new data becomes available
  to the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
- SOR/2016-141, s. 50
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.43-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 11.43"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Exemptions from Disclosure]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909277 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2016-141, s. 51(F)

\]
:::

- **[[11.44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.44
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), if an
  employer has filed a claim for exemption from the requirement to
  disclose information on a safety data sheet or on a label under
  subsection 11(2) of the [Hazardous Materials Information Review
  Act](/eng/acts/H-2.7), the employer shall disclose, in place of the
  information that the employer is exempt from disclosing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if there is no final disposition of the proceedings
    in relation to the claim, the date on which the claim for exemption
    was filed and the registry number assigned to the claim under
    section 10 of the [Hazardous Materials Information Review
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-88-456); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the final disposition of the proceedings in
    relation to the claim is that the claim is valid, a statement that
    an exemption has been granted and the date on which the exemption
    was granted.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a claim for exemption is in respect of a product
  identifier, the employer shall, on the safety data sheet or label of
  the hazardous product, disclose, in place of that product identifier,
  a code name or code number specified by the employer as the product
  identifier for that hazardous product.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
- SOR/2016-141, s. 52
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.44-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 11.44"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Hazardous Waste]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909287 .Subheading}

- **[[11.45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.45
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a hazardous product in the work
  place is hazardous waste, the employer shall disclose the generic name
  and hazard information in respect of the hazardous product by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} applying a label to the hazardous waste or its
    container; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} posting a sign in a conspicuous place near the
    hazardous waste or its container.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall provide education and training to
  employees regarding the safe storage and handling of hazardous waste.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
- SOR/2016-141, s. 52
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.45-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 11.45"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Information Required in a Medical Emergency]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909295 .Subheading}

**[[11.46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.46 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
subsection 125.2(1) of the Act, a medical professional is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a registered nurse registered or licensed under the
  laws of a province; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a medic.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 12
- SOR/94-165, s. 44
:::

### [DIVISION IV]{.HLabel2}[[\[Repealed, SOR/94-165, s. 45\]]{.Repealed}]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909301 .Subheading}

## [PART XII]{.HLabel1}[Confined Spaces]{.HTitleText1} {#h-909302 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909303 .Subheading}

**[[12.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[confined space]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a storage tank, process
vessel, ballast tank or other enclosure not designed or intended for
human occupancy, except for the purpose of performing work,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} that has poor ventilation;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in which there may be an oxygen deficient atmosphere;
  or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in which there may be an airborne hazardous
  substance. ([*espace clos*]{.otherLang lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 19
- SOR/2014-141, s. 14(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.1-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 12.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909310 .Subheading}

- **[[12.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  a person is about to enter into a confined space, the employer shall
  appoint a qualified person to verify by tests that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of any chemical agent in the
    confined space

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to which the person is likely to be exposed does
      not exceed the limit referred to in subsection 11.23(1), and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} does not exceed the limit referred to in section
      11.24;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of airborne hazardous substances,
    other than chemical agents, in the confined space is not hazardous
    to the safety or health of the person;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of oxygen in the atmosphere in
    the confined space is not less than 18% by volume and not more than
    23% by volume, at normal atmospheric pressure, and in any case the
    partial pressure of oxygen is not less than 135 mm Hg;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the requirements set out in paragraphs (a) to (c)
    are maintained during the entire period of proposed occupancy of the
    confined space by the person;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any liquid in which a person may drown or any
    free-flowing solid in which a person may become entrapped has been
    removed to the extent possible from the confined space;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the entry of any liquid, free-flowing solid or
    hazardous substance into the confined space has been prevented by a
    secure means of disconnection or the fitting of blank flanges;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} all electrical and mechanical equipment that
    presents a hazard to a person entering into, exiting from or
    occupying the confined space has been disconnected from its power
    source and locked out; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the opening for entry into and exit from the
    confined space is sufficient in size to allow safe passage of a
    person who is using protection equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The qualified person referred to in subsection (1)
  shall, in a signed written report,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} set out

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the location of the confined space,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a record of the results of the tests made in
      accordance with subsection (1), and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} an evaluation of the hazards of the confined
      space;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the employer has established procedures to be
    followed by a person entering into, exiting from or occupying the
    confined space, identify which of those procedures are to be
    followed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the employer has not established the procedures
    referred to in paragraph (b), set out the procedures to be followed
    by a person referred to in that paragraph;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} identify the protection equipment referred to in
    Part XIII that is to be used by every person granted access to the
    confined space;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} identify which of the emergency procedures are to
    be followed if the employer has established emergency procedures to
    be followed in the event of an accident or other emergency in or
    near the confined space, including immediate evacuation of the
    confined space when

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an alarm is activated, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} there is any significant change in the limit or
      concentration referred to in subsection (1);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if the employer has not established emergency
    procedures referred to in paragraph (e), set out emergency
    procedures to be followed, including immediate evacuation of the
    confined space in the circumstances referred to in that paragraph;
    and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} specify the protection equipment, emergency
    equipment and any additional equipment to be used by an employee who
    undertakes rescue operations in the event of an accident or other
    emergency.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall provide the protection equipment
  referred to in subsection (2) to each person granted access to the
  confined space.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The written report referred to in subsection (2) and
  any procedures identified in it shall be explained to an employee who
  is about to enter into the confined space, other than the qualified
  person referred to in subsection (1), and the employee shall
  acknowledge by signing a dated copy of the report that he or she has
  read it and that the report and the procedures were explained to him
  or her.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employee referred to in subsection (4) shall be
  instructed and trained in the procedures and in the use of the
  protection equipment referred to in subsection (2).

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who enters into, exits from or
  occupies the confined space shall follow the procedures and use the
  protection equipment referred to in subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 19
- SOR/94-165, s. 46
- SOR/2014-141, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.2-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 12.2"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[12.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.3 .sectionLabel}** Where conditions in
the confined space or the nature of the work to be performed in the
confined space are such that subparagraph 12.2(1)(a)(i) and paragraphs
12.2(1)(c), (e) and (f) cannot be complied with, the following
procedures shall apply:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a qualified person trained in the procedures referred
  to in subsection 12.2(2) shall be

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in attendance outside the confined space,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in communication with the person inside the
    confined space, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} provided with a suitable alarm device for
    summoning assistance;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} every person granted access to the confined space
  shall be provided with and trained in the use of the protection
  equipment referred to in subsection 12.2(2);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} every employee entering into, exiting from and
  occupying the confined space shall wear a safety harness that is
  securely attached to a life line that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is attached to a secure anchor outside the confined
    space, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is controlled by the qualified person referred to
    in paragraph (a);

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} two or more employees shall be in the immediate
  vicinity of the confined space to assist in the event of an accident
  or other emergency; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} one of the employees referred to in paragraph (d)
  shall

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} be trained in the emergency procedures referred to
    in subsection 12.2(2),

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} be a first aid attendant who has successfully
    completed a CPR course, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} be provided with the protection equipment and
    emergency equipment referred to in subsection 12.2(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 47
:::

**[[12.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.4 .sectionLabel}** Before a confined
space is sealed, the person in charge of the area surrounding the
confined space shall ascertain that no person is inside the confined
space.

### [Hot Work Operations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909359 .Subheading}

- **[[12.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Hot work shall not be performed in a
  confined space where an explosive or flammable hazardous substance may
  be present unless a qualified person has determined that the work can
  be safely performed therein.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where hot work is to be performed in a confined
  space,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a qualified person shall patrol the area
    surrounding the confined space and maintain therein a fire
    protection watch until all hazard of fire is passed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} fire extinguishers shall be provided in the area
    referred to in paragraph (a).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 19
- SOR/2014-141, s. 14(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.5-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 12.5"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Ventilation Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909367 .Subheading}

- **[[12.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  a hazardous substance is likely to be produced by hot work in a
  confined space,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the confined space shall be ventilated in
    accordance with subsection (2); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} every employee who enters into, exits from and
    occupies the confined space shall use respiratory protective
    equipment that meets the requirements of section 13.7.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the requirements set out in subsection 12.2(1)
  have been maintained with the use of ventilation equipment, no person
  shall be granted access to the confined space unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the ventilation equipment is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} equipped with an alarm that will, if the
      equipment fails, be activated automatically and be audible or
      visible to any person in the confined space, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} monitored by an employee who is in constant
      attendance at the equipment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the event of a failure of the ventilation
    equipment, sufficient time is available for the person to escape
    from the confined space before

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person's exposure to or the concentration of
      a hazardous substance in the confined space exceeds the limit
      prescribed in paragraph 12.2(1)(a),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of airborne hazardous
      substances, other than chemical agents, in the confined space is
      hazardous to the safety or health of the person, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the concentration of oxygen in the atmosphere
      ceases to meet the requirements of paragraph 12.2(1)(c).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employee referred to in subparagraph (2)(a)(ii)
  shall activate an alarm in the event of a failure of the ventilation
  equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 19
- SOR/2014-141, s. 8
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 9](#1343834-1344458){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343834-1344458.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.6-20140529.html "Link to previous version of section 12.6"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Reports and Procedures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909383 .Subheading}

**[[12.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.7 .sectionLabel}** The written report
referred to in subsection 12.2(2) shall be kept by the employer for one
year after the date on which the qualified person signs the report.

**[[12.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.8 .sectionLabel}** Where the employer
establishes procedures or emergency procedures referred to in paragraph
12.2(2)(b) or (e), he shall keep a copy of them at his place of business
nearest to the work place in which the confined space is located.

## [PART XIII]{.HLabel1}[Safety Materials, Equipment, Devices and Clothing]{.HTitleText1} {#h-909386 .Part}

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909387 .Subheading}

**[[13.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.1 .sectionLabel}** Where

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is not reasonably practicable to eliminate or
  control a safety or health hazard in a work place within safe limits,
  and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the use of protection equipment may prevent or reduce
  injury from that hazard,

every person granted access to the work place who is exposed to that
hazard shall use the protection equipment prescribed by this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 48(F)
:::

**[[13.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.2 .sectionLabel}** All protection
equipment

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall be designed to protect the person from the
  hazard for which it is provided; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall not in itself create a hazard.

**[[13.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.3 .sectionLabel}** All protection
equipment provided by the employer shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} be maintained, inspected and tested by a qualified
  person; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where necessary to prevent a health hazard, be
  maintained in a clean and sanitary condition by a qualified person.

### [Protective Headwear]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909400 .Subheading}

**[[13.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.4 .sectionLabel}** Where there is a
hazard of head injury in a work place, the employer shall provide
protective headwear that meets the standards set out in CSA Standard
Z94.1-M1977, Industrial Protective Headwear, the English version of
which is dated April 1977, as amended to September 1982, and the French
version of which is dated April 1980, as amended to September 1982.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344488){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344488.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.4-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.4"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Protective Footwear]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909402 .Subheading}

- **[[13.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where there is a hazard of a foot
  injury or electric shock through footwear in a work place, protective
  footwear that meets the standards set out in CSA Standard Z195-M1984,
  Protective Footwear, the English version of which is dated March 1984
  and the French version of which is dated December 1984, shall be used.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where there is a hazard of slipping in a work place,
  non-slip footwear shall be used.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344490){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344490.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.5-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.5"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Eye and Face Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909406 .Subheading}

**[[13.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.6 .sectionLabel}** Where there is a
hazard of injury to the eyes, face, ears or front of the neck of an
employee in a work place, the employer shall provide eye or face
protectors that meet the standards set out in CSA Standard Z94.3-M1982,
Industrial Eye and Face Protectors, the English version of which is
dated May 1982 and the French version of which is dated February 1983.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344492){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344492.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.6-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.6"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Respiratory Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909408 .Subheading}

- **[[13.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), if there
  is a risk of injury or disease due to exposure to an oxygen deficient
  atmosphere in a work place, the employer shall provide every person
  who is granted access to the work place with respiratory protective
  equipment by means of which air will be supplied and that meets the
  following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is listed in the United States National
    Institute for Occupational Safety and Health publication entitled
    Certified Equipment List, as amended from time to time; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it protects the respiratory tract against the
    oxygen deficiency.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), if there is a risk of
  injury or disease due to exposure to an airborne hazardous substance
  in a work place, the employer shall provide every person who is
  granted access to the work place with respiratory protective equipment
  that meets the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if air is supplied by means of the respiratory
    protective equipment, it is listed in the United States National
    Institute for Occupational Safety and Health publication entitled
    Certified Equipment List, as amended from time to time;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if air is not supplied by means of the respiratory
    protective equipment, it is listed

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the United States National Institute for
      Occupational Safety and Health publication entitled Certified
      Equipment List, as amended from time to time, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the CSA Group publication entitled CSA Group
      Product Listing, as amended from time to time;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for respiratory protective equipment referred to in
    subparagraph (b)(ii), it meets the requirements set out in CSA
    Standard Z94.4.1, Performance of filtering respirators, as amended
    from time to time; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it protects the respiratory tract against the
    hazardous substance.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Respiratory protective equipment referred to in
  subsection (1) or (2) shall be selected, fitted, cared for, used and
  maintained in accordance with CSA Standard Z94.4, Selection, use, and
  care of respirators, as amended from time to time.

- [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The employer must ensure that air that is supplied
  by means of respiratory protective equipment referred to in paragraph
  (1)(a) or (2)(a), and the system that supplies the air, including its
  cylinders, meet the requirements set out in CSA Standard Z180.1,
  Compressed breathing air and systems, as amended from time to time.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where there is a likelihood of exposure to
  hydrogen-sulphide gas or combustible gases at a drilling rig, drilling
  unit or production facility, the employer shall provide, at a readily
  accessible location

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on the drill floor, at least one self-contained
    positive pressure breathing device for each employee normally
    employed on the drill floor or an air manifold equipped with a face
    mask for each such employee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at least two portable hydrogen-sulphide gas
    detectors; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} at least two portable combustible gas detectors.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where employee sleeping quarters are located adjacent
  to a drilling rig or on a drilling unit or production facility, at
  least four self-contained positive pressure breathing devices shall be
  located in a readily accessible location.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-94, s. 10\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 19
- SOR/94-165, s. 49
- SOR/2014-141, s. 14(F)
- [SOR/2021-140, s. 2](#1301883-1304754){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1301883-1304754.html"}
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 10](#1343836-1344477){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343836-1344477.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.7-20220222.html "Link to previous version of section 13.7"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[13.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.8 .sectionLabel}** Where a steel or
aluminum self-contained breathing apparatus cylinder has a dent deeper
than 1.5 mm and less than 50 mm in major diameter or shows evidence of
deep isolated pitting, cracks or splits, the cylinder shall be removed
from service until it has been shown to be safe for use by means of a
hydrostatic test at a pressure equal to one and one-half times the
maximum allowable working pressure.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 50
:::

### [Skin Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909426 .Subheading}

**[[13.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.9 .sectionLabel}** Where there is a
hazard of injury or disease to or through the skin in a work place, the
employer shall provide to every person granted access to the work place

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a shield or screen;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a cream or barrier lotion to protect the skin; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an appropriate body covering.

### [Fall-Protection Systems]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909431 .Subheading}

- **[[13.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.10
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a person, other than an
  employee who is installing or removing a fall-protection system in
  accordance with the instructions referred to in subsection (5), works
  from

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an unguarded structure that is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} more than 2.4 m above the nearest permanent safe
      level,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} above any moving parts of machinery or any other
      surface or thing that could cause injury to an employee upon
      contact,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} above an open hopper, vat or pit, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} above water more than 1 m deep, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a ladder at a height of more than 2.4 m above the
    nearest permanent safe level where, because of the nature of the
    work, that person can use only one hand to hold onto the ladder,

  the employer shall provide a fall-protection system.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The components of a fall-protection system shall meet
  the following standards:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard Z259.1-1976, Fall-Arresting Safety
    Belts and Lanyards for the Construction and Mining Industries, the
    English version of which is dated November 1976, as amended to May
    1979 and the French version of which is dated April 1980;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard Z259.2-M1979, Fall-Arresting Devices,
    Personnel Lowering Devices and Life Lines, the English version of
    which is dated November 1979 and the French version of which is
    dated October 1983; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} CSA Standard Z259.3-M1978, Lineman's Body Belt and
    Lineman's Safety Strap, the English version of which is dated
    September 1978, as amended to April 1981, and the French version of
    which is dated April 1980, as amended to April 1981.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The anchor of a fall-protection system shall be
  capable of withstanding a force of 17.8 kN.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A fall-protection system that is used to arrest the
  fall of a person shall prevent that person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} from being subjected to a peak fall arrest force
    greater than 8 kN; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} from falling freely for more than 1.2 m.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every employee required to install or remove a
  fall-protection system in a work place shall be instructed and trained
  by the employer in the procedures to be followed for the installation
  or removal of the system.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344494){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344494.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.10-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 13.10"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Emergency Escape Devices]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909450 .Subheading}

- **[[13.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where practicable, an emergency
  escape device that is equipped with a brake mechanism that controls
  the descent of persons using the device shall be provided in the
  derrick of a drilling rig or an elevated part of a production
  facility.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall set out in writing working
  instructions for the use of the device referred to in subsection (1).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The instructions referred to in subsection (2) shall
  be kept in a conspicuous place on the drilling rig or production
  facility.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} An emergency escape device referred to in
  subsection (1) shall be installed, inspected and maintained by a
  qualified person.

### [Protection Against Drowning]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909456 .Subheading}

- **[[13.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where, in a work place, there is a
  hazard of drowning, the employer shall provide every person granted
  access to the work place with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a life jacket or personal flotation device that
    meets the standards set out in the Canadian General Standards Board
    Standard

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} CAN2-65.7-M80, Life Jackets, Inherently Buoyant
      Type, dated April 1980, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 65-GP-11, Standard for: Personal Flotation
      Devices, dated October 1972; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a safety net or a fall-protection system.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, in a work place, there is a hazard of
  drowning,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} emergency equipment shall be provided and held in
    readiness;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a qualified person to operate all the emergency
    equipment provided shall be readily available;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if appropriate, a powered rescue boat shall be
    provided and held in readiness; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} written emergency procedures shall be prepared by
    the employer containing

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a full description of the procedures to be
      followed and the responsibilities of all persons granted access to
      the work place, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the location of any emergency equipment.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a work place is a wharf, dock, pier, quay or
  other similar structure, a ladder that extends at least two rungs
  below water level shall, where reasonably practicable, be installed on
  the face of the structure every 60 m along its length.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 51(F)
:::

### [Loose-fitting Clothing]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909473 .Subheading}

**[[13.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.13 .sectionLabel}** Loose-fitting
clothing, long hair, dangling accessories, jewellery or other similar
items that are likely to be hazardous to the safety or health of an
employee in a work place shall not be worn unless they are so tied,
covered or otherwise secured as to prevent the hazard.

### [Protection from Extreme Temperatures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909475 .Subheading}

**[[13.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.14 .sectionLabel}** Where there is a
likelihood that exposure of an employee to extreme temperatures could
result in the employee suffering from hypothermia or hyperthermia,
protection equipment suitable to protect the employee from the hazard
shall be used.

### [Protection Against Moving Vehicles]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909477 .Subheading}

**[[13.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.15 .sectionLabel}** Where an employee
is regularly exposed to a hazard resulting from contact with moving
vehicles during his work, he shall wear a high-visibility vest or other
high-visibility clothing.

### [Fire Protection Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909479 .Subheading}

- **[[13.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), every
  onshore drilling rig shall be equipped with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at least one portable fire extinguisher with a 40
    BC rating, as defined in the ULC Standard, that is readily
    accessible from

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} each boiler,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the drill floor or doghouse,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the enclosure for the choke manifold,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} every enclosure housing a fuel-fired engine or
      heating unit, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} every welding unit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at least one portable multipurpose fire
    extinguisher with an 80 BC rating, as defined in the ULC Standard.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Fire protection equipment shall be installed,
  inspected and maintained for every work place onshore in accordance
  with the standards set out in Parts 6 and 7 of the National Fire Code.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of interpreting the standards
  referred to in subsection (2), "acceptable" means appropriate.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every work place shall be equipped with the fire
  protection equipment that is appropriate for fighting any class of
  fire that may occur.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} No person shall tamper with or activate without cause
  any fire protection equipment.

**[[13.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.17 .sectionLabel}** All fire protection
equipment shall be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} inspected by a qualified person at least once a
  month; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} tested, maintained and repaired by a qualified
  person.

### [Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909496 .Subheading}

- **[[13.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A record of all protection equipment
  provided by the employer and requiring maintenance shall be kept for
  as long as the equipment is in use.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The record referred to in subsection (1) shall
  contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the protection equipment and the
    date of its acquisition by the employer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date and result of each inspection and test of
    the protection equipment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the date and nature of any maintenance work
    performed on the protection equipment since its acquisition by the
    employer; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name of the qualified person who performed the
    inspection, test, maintenance or repair of the protection equipment.

### [Instructions and Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909504 .Subheading}

- **[[13.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.19
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person granted access to the
  work place who uses protection equipment shall be instructed by the
  employer in the use of the equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who uses protection equipment shall be
  instructed and trained in the use, operation and maintenance of the
  equipment.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person granted access to a work place shall be
  instructed in respect of the written emergency procedures referred to
  in paragraph 13.12(2)(d).

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} set out in writing and keep readily available for
    examination by the employees referred to in subsection (2), the
    instructions referred to in that subsection; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} keep readily available for examination by every
    person granted access to the work place a copy of the emergency
    procedures referred to in paragraph 13.12(2)(d).

### [Defective Protection Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909512 .Subheading}

**[[13.20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.20 .sectionLabel}** Where an employee
finds any defect in protection equipment that may render it unsafe for
use, he shall report the defect to his employer as soon as possible.

**[[13.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.21 .sectionLabel}** An employer shall
mark or tag as unsafe and remove from service any protection equipment
that has a defect that may render it unsafe for use.

## [PART XIV]{.HLabel1}[Tools and Machinery]{.HTitleText1} {#h-909515 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909516 .Subheading}

**[[14.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[explosive actuated fastening tool]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a tool
that, by means of an explosive force, propels or discharges a fastener
for the purpose of impinging it on, affixing it to or causing it to
penetrate another object or material. ([*pistolet de scellement à
cartouches explosives*]{.otherLang lang="fr"})

### [Design, Construction, Operation and Use of Tools]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909518 .Subheading}

**[[14.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.2 .sectionLabel}** The exterior surface
of any tool used by an employee in a fire hazard area shall be made of
non-sparking material.

**[[14.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.3 .sectionLabel}** All portable electric
tools used by employees shall meet the standards set out in CSA Standard
CAN/CSA-C22.2 No. 71.1-M89, Portable Electric Tools, the English version
of which is dated September 1989 and the French version of which is
dated February 1991.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 52
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344495){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344495.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.3"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[14.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), all
  portable electric tools used by employees shall be grounded.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to tools that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are powered by a self-contained battery;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have a protective system of double insulation; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} are used in a location where reliable grounding
    cannot be obtained if the tools are supplied from a double insulated
    portable ground fault circuit interrupter of the class A type that
    meets the standards set out in CSA Standard C22.2 No. 144-1977,
    Ground Fault Circuit Interrupters, dated March 1977.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344497){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344497.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.4-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.4"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[14.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.5 .sectionLabel}** All portable electric
tools used by employees in a fire hazard area shall be marked as
appropriate for use or designed for use in such a fire hazard area.

**[[14.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.6 .sectionLabel}** Where an air hose is
connected to a portable air-powered tool used by an employee, a
restraining device shall be attached

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to all hose connections; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where an employee may be injured by the tool falling,
  to the tool.

<!-- -->

- **[[14.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All explosive actuated fastening
  tools used by employees shall meet the standards set out in CSA
  Standard Z166-1975, Explosive Actuated Fastening Tools, dated June
  1975.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No employee shall operate an explosive actuated
  fastening tool unless authorized to do so by his employer.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who operates an explosive actuated
  fastening tool shall operate it in accordance with the CSA Standard
  referred to in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344499){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344499.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.7-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.7"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[14.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.8 .sectionLabel}** All chain saws used
by employees shall meet the standards set out in CSA Standard
CAN3-Z62.1-M85, Chain Saws, dated February 1985.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344501){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344501.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.8-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.8"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Defective Tools and Machines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909538 .Subheading}

**[[14.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.9 .sectionLabel}** Where an employee
finds any defect in a tool or machine that may render it unsafe for use,
he shall report the defect to his employer as soon as possible.

**[[14.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.10 .sectionLabel}** An employer shall
mark or tag as unsafe and remove from service any tool or machine used
by his employees that has a defect that may render it unsafe for use.

### [Instructions and Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909541 .Subheading}

**[[14.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.11 .sectionLabel}** Every employee
shall be instructed and trained by a qualified person appointed by his
employer in the safe and proper inspection, maintenance and use of all
tools and machinery that he is required to use.

- **[[14.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall maintain a
  manual of operating instructions for each type of portable electric
  tool, portable air-powered tool, explosive actuated fastening tool and
  machine used by his employees.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A manual referred to in subsection (1) shall be kept
  by the employer readily available for examination by an employee who
  is required to use the tool or machine to which the manual applies.

### [General Requirements for Machine Guards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909546 .Subheading}

- **[[14.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every machine that has exposed
  moving, rotating, electrically charged or hot parts or that processes,
  transports or handles material that constitutes a hazard to an
  employee shall be equipped with a machine guard that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevents the employee or any part of his body from
    coming into contact with the parts or material;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevents access by the employee to the area of
    exposure to the hazard during the operation of the machine; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} renders the machine inoperative if the employee or
    any part of his clothing is in or near a part of the machine that is
    likely to cause injury.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} So far as is reasonably practicable, a machine guard
  referred to in subsection (1) shall not be removable.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A machine guard shall be so constructed, installed
  and maintained that it meets the requirements of subsection (1).

### [Use, Operation, Repair and Maintenance of Machine Guards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909554 .Subheading}

**[[14.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.14 .sectionLabel}** Machine guards
shall be operated, maintained and repaired by a qualified person.

- **[[14.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), where a
  machine guard is installed on a machine, no person shall use or
  operate the machine unless the machine guard is in its proper
  position.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A machine may be operated when the machine guard is
  not in its proper position in order to permit the removal of an
  injured person from the machine.

<!-- -->

- **[[14.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), where it
  is necessary to remove a machine guard from a machine in order to
  perform repair or maintenance work on the machine, no person shall
  perform the repair or maintenance work unless the machine has been
  rendered inoperative.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where it is not reasonably practicable to render a
  machine referred to in subsection (1) inoperative in order to perform
  repair or maintenance work on the machine, the work may be performed
  if the person performing the work is a qualified person.

### [Abrasive Wheels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909562 .Subheading}

**[[14.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.17 .sectionLabel}** Abrasive wheels
shall be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} used only on machines equipped with machine guards,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} mounted between flanges, and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} operated

in accordance with sections 4 to 6 of CSA Standard B173.5-1979, Safety
Requirements for the Use, Care and Protection of Abrasive Wheels, dated
February 1979.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344503){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344503.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.17-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.17"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[14.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.18 .sectionLabel}** A bench grinder
shall be equipped with a work rest or other device that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevents the work piece from jamming between the
  abrasive wheel and the wheel guard; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} does not make contact with the abrasive wheel at any
  time.

### [Mechanical Power Transmission Apparatus]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909571 .Subheading}

**[[14.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.19 .sectionLabel}** Equipment used in
the mechanical transmission of power shall be guarded in accordance with
sections 7 to 10 of ANSI Standard ANSI B15.1-1972, Safety Standard for
Mechanical Power Transmission Apparatus, dated July 1972.

### [Woodworking Machinery]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909573 .Subheading}

**[[14.20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.20 .sectionLabel}** Woodworking
machinery shall be guarded in accordance with clause 3.3 of CSA Standard
Z114-M1977, Safety Code for the Woodworking Industry, dated March 1977.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344505){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344505.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.20-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.20"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Punch Presses]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909575 .Subheading}

**[[14.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.21 .sectionLabel}** Punch presses shall
meet the standards set out in CSA Standard Z142-1976, Code for the
Guarding of Punch Presses at Point of Operation, the English version of
which is dated February 1976 and the French version of which is dated
February 1977.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 53
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344506){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344506.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.21-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 14.21"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART XV]{.HLabel1}[Materials Handling]{.HTitleText1} {#h-909579 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909580 .Subheading}

**[[15.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[materials handling area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[materials handling area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area within
    which materials handling equipment may create a hazard to any
    person; ([aire de manutention des matériaux]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[materials handling equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[materials handling equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means equipment
    used to transport, lift, move or position materials, goods or things
    and includes mobile equipment but does not include an elevating
    device; ([appareil de manutention des matériaux]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[operator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[operator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employee who operates
    materials handling equipment; ([conducteur]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[safe working load]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[safe working load]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, with respect to
    materials handling equipment, the maximum load that the materials
    handling equipment is designed and constructed to handle or support
    safely; ([charge de travail admissible]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[signaller]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[signaller]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person instructed by an
    employer to direct, by means of visual or auditory signals, the safe
    movement and operation of materials handling equipment.
    ([signaleur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909587 .Subheading}

**[[15.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.2 .sectionLabel}** This Part does not
apply to or in respect of the use and operation of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} motor vehicles on public roads; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} tackle used in the loading or unloading of ships.

### [DIVISION I]{.HLabel2}[Design and Construction]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909591 .Subheading}

#### [Standards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909592 .Subheading}

- **[[15.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The design and construction of
  drilling and production hoisting equipment shall meet the standards
  set out in API Standard API SPEC 8A, API Specification for Drilling
  and Production Hoisting Equipment, Eleventh Edition, dated May 1,
  1985.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The design and construction of offshore cranes shall
  meet the standards set out in API Standard API Spec 2C, API
  Specification for Offshore Cranes, Third Edition, dated March 1983 as
  amended to May 1984.

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909596 .Subheading}

- **[[15.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Materials handling equipment shall,
  so far as is reasonably practicable, be so designed and constructed
  that if there is a failure of any part of the materials handling
  equipment, it will not result in loss of control of the materials
  handling equipment or create a hazardous condition.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} All glass in doors, windows and other parts of
  materials handling equipment shall be of a type that will not shatter
  into sharp or dangerous pieces on impact.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 54(F)
:::

#### [Protection from Falling Objects]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909602 .Subheading}

- **[[15.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where materials handling equipment
  is used under such circumstances that the operator may be struck by a
  falling object or shifting load, the employer shall equip the
  materials handling equipment with a protective structure of such a
  design, construction and strength that it will, under all foreseeable
  conditions, prevent the penetration of the object or load into the
  area occupied by the operator.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A protective structure referred to in subsection (1)
  shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} constructed from non-combustible or fire resistant
    material; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} designed to permit quick exit from the materials
    handling equipment in an emergency.

**[[15.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.6 .sectionLabel}** Where, during the
loading or unloading of materials handling equipment, the load will pass
over the operator's position, the operator shall not occupy the
materials handling equipment unless it is equipped with a protective
structure referred to in section 15.5.

#### [Protection from Overturning]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909609 .Subheading}

- **[[15.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where mobile equipment is used in
  circumstances where it may turn over, it shall be fitted with a
  rollover protection device that meets the standards set out in CSA
  Standard B352-M1980, Rollover Protective Structures (ROPS) for
  Agricultural, Construction, Eathmoving, Forestry, Industrial and
  Mining Machines, the English version of which is dated September 1980
  and the French version of which is dated April 1991.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Guards shall be installed on the deck of every
  drilling unit, production facility and elevated working area on which
  mobile equipment is used to prevent the equipment from falling over
  the sides of the deck or area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 55
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344507){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344507.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.7-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 15.7"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Fuel Tanks]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909615 .Subheading}

**[[15.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.8 .sectionLabel}** Where a fuel tank,
compressed gas cylinder or similar container contains a hazardous
substance and is mounted on materials handling equipment, it shall be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} so located or protected that under all conditions it
  is not hazardous to the safety or health of an employee who is
  required to operate or ride on the materials handling equipment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} connected to fuel overflow and vent pipes that are so
  located that fuel spills and vapours cannot

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} be ignited by hot exhaust pipes or other hot or
    sparking parts, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} be hazardous to the safety or health of any
    employee who is required to operate or ride on the materials
    handling equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 19
- SOR/94-165, s. 56(F)
- SOR/2014-141, s. 14(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.8-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 15.8"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Protection from Environmental Conditions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909623 .Subheading}

- **[[15.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Materials handling equipment that is
  regularly used outdoors shall be fitted with a roof or other structure
  that will protect the operator from exposure to any environmental
  condition that is likely to be hazardous to his safety or health.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where heat produced by materials handling equipment
  is capable of raising the temperature in any area occupied by an
  employee on the equipment to 27°C or more, the area shall be protected
  from the heat by an insulated barrier.

#### [Vibration]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909627 .Subheading}

**[[15.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.10 .sectionLabel}** All materials
handling equipment shall be so designed and constructed that the
operator will not be injured or his control of the materials handling
equipment impaired by any vibration, jolting or other uneven movement of
the materials handling equipment.

#### [Controls]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909629 .Subheading}

**[[15.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.11 .sectionLabel}** The arrangement and
design of dial displays and the controls and general layout and design
of the operator's compartment or position on all materials handling
equipment shall not hinder or prevent the operator from operating the
materials handling equipment.

#### [Fire Extinguishers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909631 .Subheading}

- **[[15.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Mobile equipment that is used or
  operated for transporting or handling combustible or flammable
  substances shall be equipped with a portable dry chemical fire
  extinguisher.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The fire extinguisher referred to in subsection (1)
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have not less than a 5B rating, as defined in the
    ULC Standard; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be so located that it is readily accessible to the
    operator while he is in the operating position.

#### [Means of Entering and Exiting]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909637 .Subheading}

**[[15.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.13 .sectionLabel}** All materials
handling equipment shall be provided with a step, handhold or other
means of entering into and exiting from the compartment or position of
the operator and any other place on the equipment that an employee
enters in order to service the equipment.

#### [Lighting]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909639 .Subheading}

**[[15.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.14 .sectionLabel}** Where mobile
equipment is used or operated by an employee in a work place at night or
at any time when the level of lighting within the work place is less
than 1 dalx, the mobile equipment shall be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} fitted on the front and rear thereof with warning
  lights that are visible from a distance of not less than 100 m; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} provided with lighting that ensures the safe
  operation of the equipment under all conditions of use.

#### [Control Systems]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909643 .Subheading}

**[[15.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.15 .sectionLabel}** All mobile
equipment shall be fitted with braking, steering and other control
systems that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} are capable of safely controlling and stopping the
  movement of the mobile equipment and any hoist, bucket or other part
  of the mobile equipment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} respond reliably and quickly to moderate effort on
  the part of the operator.

**[[15.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.16 .sectionLabel}** Any mobile
equipment that is normally used for transporting employees from place to
place in a work place shall be equipped with

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a mechanical parking brake; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a hydraulic or pneumatic braking system.

#### [Warnings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909650 .Subheading}

**[[15.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.17 .sectionLabel}** Mobile equipment
shall be fitted with a horn or similar audible warning device having a
distinctive sound that can be clearly heard above the noise of the
equipment and any surrounding noise.

#### [Seat Belts]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909652 .Subheading}

**[[15.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.18 .sectionLabel}** Where mobile
equipment is used under conditions where a seat belt or shoulder strap
type restraining device is likely to contribute to the safety of the
operator or passengers, the mobile equipment shall be fitted with such a
belt or device.

#### [Rear View Mirror]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909654 .Subheading}

**[[15.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.19 .sectionLabel}** Where mobile
equipment cannot be operated safely in reverse unless it is equipped
with a rear view mirror, the mobile equipment shall be so equipped.

#### [Electric Materials Handling Equipment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909656 .Subheading}

**[[15.20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.20 .sectionLabel}** Any materials
handling equipment that is electrically powered shall be so designed and
constructed that the operator and all other employees are protected from
electrical shock or injury by means of protective guards, screens or
panels secured by bolts, screws or other equally reliable fasteners.

#### [Automatic Materials Handling Equipment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909658 .Subheading}

**[[15.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.21 .sectionLabel}** Where materials
handling equipment that is controlled or operated by a remote or
automatic system may make physical contact with an employee, it shall be
prevented from doing so by the provision of an emergency stop system or
barricades.

#### [Conveyors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909660 .Subheading}

**[[15.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.22 .sectionLabel}** The design,
construction, installation, operation and maintenance of each conveyor,
cableway or other similar materials handling equipment shall meet the
standards set out in ASME Standard ANSI/ASME B20.1-1984, Safety
Standards for Conveyors and Related Equipment, dated May 31, 1984.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 57
:::

### [DIVISION II]{.HLabel2}[Maintenance, Operation and Use]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909664 .Subheading}

#### [Inspection, Testing and Maintenance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909665 .Subheading}

- **[[15.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.23
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before materials handling equipment
  is operated for the first time in a work place, the employer shall set
  out in writing instructions for the inspection, testing and
  maintenance of that materials handling equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The instructions referred to in subsection (1) shall,
  subject to section 15.25, specify the nature and frequency of
  inspections, tests and maintenance.

<!-- -->

- **[[15.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.24
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every inspection, test and
  maintenance of materials handling equipment shall be performed by a
  qualified person.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The qualified person referred to in subsection (1)
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} comply with the instructions referred to in
    subsection 15.23(1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} make and sign a report of each inspection, test or
    maintenance work performed by him.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The report referred to in paragraph (2)(b) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} include the date of the inspection, test or
    maintenance performed by the qualified person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify the materials handling equipment that was
    inspected, tested or maintained; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the safety observations of the qualified
    person inspecting, testing or maintaining the materials handling
    equipment.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall keep at the work place at which
  the materials handling equipment is located a copy of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the instructions referred to in subsection
    15.23(1), for as long as the materials handling equipment is in use;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the report referred to in paragraph (2)(b) for one
    year after the report is signed.

<!-- -->

- **[[15.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.25
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The operation, maintenance and
  inspection of all draw works and associated equipment shall meet the
  standards set out in the API Standard API RP 8B, API Recommended
  Practice for Hoisting Tool Inspection and Maintenance Procedures,
  Fourth Edition, dated April 1979.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operation, maintenance and inspection of offshore
  cranes shall meet the standards set out in the API Standard API RP 2D
  API Recommended Practice for Operation and Maintenance of Offshore
  Cranes, Second Edition, dated June 20, 1984.

#### [Ropes, Slings and Chains]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909684 .Subheading}

**[[15.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.26 .sectionLabel}** The employer shall,
with respect to the use and maintenance of any rope or sling or any
attachment or fitting thereon used by an employee, adopt and implement
the recommendations set out in chapter 5 of the Accident Prevention
Manual for Industrial Operations, Engineering and Technology, 8th
Edition, published by the National Safety Council of the United States,
dated 1980.

**[[15.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.27 .sectionLabel}** The employer shall,
with respect to the use and maintenance of any chain used by an
employee, adopt and implement the code of practice set out in CSA
Standard B75-1947, Code of Practice for the Use and Care of Chain, dated
May 1947.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-94, s. 11(F)](#1343840-1344509){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1343840-1344509.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.27-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 15.27"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Training]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909687 .Subheading}

- **[[15.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.28
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every operator shall be instructed
  and trained by the employer in the procedures to be followed for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the inspection of the materials handling equipment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the fuelling of the materials handling equipment,
    where applicable; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the safe and proper use of the materials handling
    equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall keep a record of any instruction
  or training given to an operator for as long as the operator remains
  in his employ.

#### [Operation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909694 .Subheading}

**[[15.29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.29 .sectionLabel}** No employer shall
require an employee to operate materials handling equipment unless the
employee is a qualified person.

- **[[15.30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.30
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No person shall operate materials
  handling equipment unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} he has a clear and unobstructed view of the area in
    which the equipment is being operated; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} he is directed by a signaller.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No materials handling equipment shall be used on a
  ramp with a slope greater than the maximum slope recommended by the
  manufacturer of the equipment.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person shall leave mobile equipment unattended
  unless the equipment has been properly secured to prevent it from
  moving.

<!-- -->

- **[[15.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.31
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall establish a
  code of signals for the purposes of paragraph 15.30(1)(b) and shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} instruct every signaller and operator employed by
    him in the use of the code; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} keep a copy of the code in a place where it is
    readily available for examination by the signallers, operators and
    other employees.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No signaller shall perform duties other than
  signalling while any materials handling equipment under his direction
  is in motion.

<!-- -->

- **[[15.32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.32
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), where it
  is not practicable for a signaller to use visual signals, a telephone,
  radio or other signalling device shall be provided by the employer for
  the use of the signaller.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No radio transmitting equipment shall be used in any
  work place for the transmission of signals when such use may activate
  electric blasting equipment in that place.

#### [Repairs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909710 .Subheading}

- **[[15.33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.33
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), any
  repair, modification or replacement of a part of any materials
  handling equipment shall not decrease the safety of the materials
  handling equipment or part.
