- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a part of less strength or quality than the
  original part is used in the repair, modification or replacement of a
  part of any materials handling equipment, the use of the materials
  handling equipment shall be restricted by the employer to such loading
  and use as will ensure the retention of the original safety of the
  equipment or part.

#### [Transporting and Positioning Employees]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909714 .Subheading}

- **[[15.34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.34
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Materials handling equipment shall
  not be used for transporting an employee unless the equipment is
  specifically designed for that purpose.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Materials handling equipment shall not be used for
  positioning an employee unless the equipment is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} equipped with a platform, bucket or basket designed
    for that purpose; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provided with a fail-safe control system that will
    prevent a free fall of the load that is carried.

#### [Loading, Unloading and Maintenance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909720 .Subheading}

**[[15.35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.35 .sectionLabel}** No materials, goods
or things shall be picked up from or placed on any mobile equipment
while the equipment is in motion unless the equipment is specifically
designed for that purpose.

**[[15.36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.36 .sectionLabel}** Except in the case
of an emergency, no employee shall get on or off any mobile equipment
while it is in motion.

- **[[15.37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.37
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no
  repair, maintenance or cleaning work shall be performed on any
  materials handling equipment while the materials handling equipment is
  being operated.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Fixed parts of materials handling equipment may be
  repaired, maintained or cleaned while the materials handling equipment
  is being operated if they are so isolated or protected that the
  operation of the materials handling equipment does not affect the
  safety of the employee performing the repair, maintenance or cleaning
  work.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 58(E)
:::

#### [Positioning the Load]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909728 .Subheading}

**[[15.38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.38 .sectionLabel}** Where mobile
equipment is travelling with a raised or suspended load, the operator
shall ensure that the load is carried as close to the ground, floor or
deck as the situation permits and in no case shall the load be carried
at a point above the centre of gravity of the loaded mobile equipment.

#### [Tools]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909730 .Subheading}

**[[15.39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.39 .sectionLabel}** Where tools, tool
boxes or spare parts are carried on materials handling equipment, they
shall be securely stored.

#### [Housekeeping]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909732 .Subheading}

**[[15.40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.40 .sectionLabel}** The floor, cab and
other occupied parts of materials handling equipment shall be kept free
of any grease, oil, materials, tools or equipment that may cause a
hazard to an employee.

#### [Parking]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909734 .Subheading}

**[[15.41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.41 .sectionLabel}** No mobile equipment
shall be parked in any place where it may interfere with the safe
movement of persons, materials, goods or things.

#### [Materials Handling Area]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909736 .Subheading}

- **[[15.42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.42
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The main approaches to any materials
  handling area shall be posted with warning signs or shall be under the
  control of a signaller while operations are in progress.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall enter a materials handling area while
  operations are in progress unless that person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is the Head of Compliance and Enforcement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is an employee whose presence in the materials
    handling area is essential to the conduct, supervision or safety of
    the operations; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is a person who has been instructed by the employer
    to be in the materials handling area while operations are in
    progress.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If any person other than a person referred to in
  subsection (2) enters a materials handling area while operations are
  in progress, the employer shall cause the operations in that area to
  be immediately discontinued and not resumed until that person has left
  the area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-148, s. 25
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 9](#1295161-1312183){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295161-1312183.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.42-20141031.html "Link to previous version of section 15.42"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Dumping]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909746 .Subheading}

**[[15.43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.43 .sectionLabel}** Where mobile
equipment designed for dumping is used to discharge a load that may
cause the mobile equipment to tip,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a bumping block shall be used, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a signaller shall give directions to the operator

to prevent the mobile equipment from tipping.

#### [Enclosed Work Place]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909751 .Subheading}

**[[15.44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.44 .sectionLabel}** Every enclosed work
place in which materials handling equipment that is powered by an
internal combustion engine is used shall be ventilated in such a manner
that the carbon monoxide concentration in the atmosphere of the work
place does not exceed the limit prescribed in section 11.23.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-141, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.44-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 15.44"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Fuelling]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909755 .Subheading}

**[[15.45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.45 .sectionLabel}** Where materials
handling equipment is fuelled in a work place, the fuelling shall be
done in accordance with the instructions given by the employer pursuant
to section 15.23 in a place where the vapours from the fuel are readily
dissipated.

#### [Cranes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909757 .Subheading}

**[[15.46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.46 .sectionLabel}** No person shall
operate a crane under conditions that are likely to create a hazard to
any person, ship, aircraft, vehicle, load or structure or to the
stability of the crane.

- **[[15.47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.47
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every crane shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have posted inside the crane control cab a load
    capacity chart that specifies the boom angle and safe working load
    for each block;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be equipped with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} boom and block travel limiting devices, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the load rating of the crane is more than
      5 t, a load measure device for the main block.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} All crane hooks shall be equipped with safety
  catches.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person shall move a crane in the vicinity of a
  helicopter deck when a helicopter is landing or taking off.

<!-- -->

- **[[15.48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.48
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Tag lines shall be used to control
  any swinging of a load that is being lifted by a crane except where
  the use of the lines may be hazardous to the safety of any person.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Loads shall not be left hanging by a crane above the
  deck of a drilling unit or production facility unless the crane
  operator is at the controls of the crane.

#### [Safe Working Loads]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909770 .Subheading}

- **[[15.49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.49
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No materials handling equipment
  shall be used or operated with a load that is in excess of its safe
  working load.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The safe working load of materials handling equipment
  shall be clearly marked on the equipment or on a label securely
  attached to a permanent part of the equipment in a position where the
  mark or label can be easily read by the operator.

#### [Aisles and Corridors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909774 .Subheading}

**[[15.50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.50 .sectionLabel}** At blind corners,
mirrors shall be installed that permit an operator to see a pedestrian,
vehicle or mobile equipment approaching the blind corner.

#### [Clearances]{.HTitleText3} {#h-909776 .Subheading}

**[[15.51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.51 .sectionLabel}** On any route that
is frequently travelled by mobile equipment, the overhead and side
clearances shall be adequate to permit the mobile equipment and its load
to be manoeuvred safely by an operator.

- **[[15.52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.52
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no
  materials handling equipment shall be operated in an area in which it
  may contact an electrical cable, a pipeline, part of a structure or
  other hazard known to the employer, unless the operator and signaller,
  if any, have been

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} warned of the presence of the hazard;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} informed of the location of the hazard; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} informed of the safety clearance that must be
    maintained with respect to the hazard in order to avoid accidental
    contact with it.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an employer is unable to determine with
  reasonable certainty the location of the hazard or the safety
  clearance referred to in subsection (1), every electrical cable shall
  be de-energized and every pipeline containing a hazardous substance
  shall be shut down and drained before any operation involving the use
  of materials handling equipment commences within the area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 19
- SOR/2014-141, s. 14(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.52-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 15.52"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION III]{.HLabel2}[Manual Handling of Materials]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909786 .Subheading}

**[[15.53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.53 .sectionLabel}** Where, because of
the weight, size, shape, toxicity or other characteristic of materials,
goods or things, the manual handling of the materials, goods or things
may be hazardous to the safety or health of an employee, the employer
shall issue instructions that the materials, goods or things shall,
where reasonably practicable, not be handled manually.

**[[15.54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.54 .sectionLabel}** Where an employee
is required to lift or carry a load in excess of 10 kg manually, the
employee shall be instructed and trained by the employer in a safe
method of lifting and carrying that load.

### [DIVISION IV]{.HLabel2}[Storage of Materials]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909789 .Subheading}

- **[[15.55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.55
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All materials, goods and things
  shall be stored and placed in such a manner that the maximum safe
  load-carrying capacity of the floor or other supporting structures is
  not exceeded.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No materials, goods or things shall be stored or
  placed in a manner that may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} obstruct or encroach on passageways, traffic lanes
    or exits;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} impede the safe operation of materials handling
    equipment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} obstruct the ready access to or the use and
    operation of firefighting equipment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} interfere with the operation of fixed fire
    protection equipment; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} be hazardous to the safety or health of any
    employee.

## [PART XVI]{.HLabel1}[Hazardous Occurrence Investigation, Recording and Reporting]{.HTitleText1} {#h-909798 .Part}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/94-165, s. 59(F)

\]
:::

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909801 .Subheading}

**[[16.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[disabling injury]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[disabling injury]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employment injury
    or occupational illness that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevents an employee from reporting for work or
      from effectively performing all the duties connected with their
      regular work on any day after the day on which the injury or
      illness occurred, whether or not that later day is a working day
      for that employee,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} results in the loss by an employee of a body
      member or part thereof or in the complete loss of the usefulness
      of a body member or part thereof, or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} results in the permanent impairment of a body
      function of an employee; ([blessure invalidante]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[minor injury]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[minor injury]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employment injury or
    an occupational illness for which medical treatment is provided and
    excludes a disabling injury. ([blessure légère]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 60
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 22](#1520605-1525068){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520605-1525068.html"}
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 27(E)](#1520620-1525127){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520620-1525127.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16.1-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 16.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1282804 .Subheading}

**[[16.1.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.1.1 .sectionLabel}** This Part does
not apply in respect of occurrences of harassment and violence in the
work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2020-130, s. 46](#1251041-1282807){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1251041-1282807.html"}
:::

### [Report by Employee]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909810 .Subheading}

**[[16.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.2 .sectionLabel}** Where an employee
becomes aware of an accident or other occurrence arising in the course
of or in connection with his work that has caused injury to him or to
any other person, he shall without delay report the accident or other
occurrence to his employer, orally or in writing.

### [Investigation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909812 .Subheading}

- **[[16.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an employer is aware of an
  accident, occupational illness or other hazardous occurrence affecting
  any of his employees in the course of employment, the employer shall,
  without delay,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} take necessary measures to prevent a recurrence of
    the hazardous occurrence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} appoint a qualified person to carry out an
    investigation of the hazardous occurrence; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} notify the safety and health committee or the
    safety and health representative, if either exists, of the hazardous
    occurrence and of the name of the person appointed to investigate
    it.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the investigation referred to in
  paragraph (1)(b), where the hazardous occurrence referred to in
  subsection (1) is an accident involving a ship or aircraft or a motor
  vehicle on a public road, the employer shall investigate the accident
  by obtaining from the appropriate police or other investigating
  authority a copy of the report made by that authority in respect of
  the accident.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} As soon as possible after receipt of the report
  referred to in subsection (2), the employer shall provide a copy
  thereof to the safety and health committee or the safety and health
  representative, if either exists.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 61
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 27(E)](#1520620-1525132){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520620-1525132.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16.3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 16.3"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Hazardous Occurrence Report]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909822 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/94-165, s. 62(F)

\]
:::

- **[[16.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall report, by the
  most rapid means of communication available to the employer, the date,
  time, location and nature of any accident, occupational illness or
  other hazardous occurrence referred to in section 16.3 to the Head of
  Compliance and Enforcement and to the safety and health committee or
  the safety and health representative, if either exists, as soon as
  feasible but not later than 24 hours after becoming aware of the
  occurrence, if the occurrence resulted in one of the following
  circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the death of an employee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a missing person;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a disabling injury to an employee;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the implementation of emergency rescue, revival or
    evacuation procedures;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a fire or explosion that threatened the safety or
    health of an employee;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the free fall of an elevating device that rendered
    the elevating device unsafe for use by an employee;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} an accidental accumulation, spill or leak of a
    hazardous substance; or

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the loss of or damage to support craft.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A written report of the accident, occupational
  illness or other hazardous occurrence referred to in subsection (1)
  shall be submitted by the employer within 14 days after the occurrence
  to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Head of Compliance and Enforcement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the safety and health committee or the safety and
    health representative, if either exists.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The report referred to in subsection (2) shall be in
  the form set out in Schedule I to this Part and contain the
  information required by the form.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 19
- SOR/94-165, s. 63
- SOR/2014-141, s. 14(F)
- SOR/2014-148, s. 26
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 9](#1295161-1312186){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295161-1312186.html"}
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 27(E)](#1520620-1525133){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520620-1525133.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16.4-20210701.html "Link to previous version of section 16.4"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[16.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.5 .sectionLabel}** If an investigation
referred to in subsection 16.3(2) reveals that the accident resulted in
a circumstance referred to in subsection 16.4(1), the employer shall,
within 14 days after the receipt of the report of the accident made by
the police or other investigating authority, submit a copy of the report
to the Head of Compliance and Enforcement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-148, s. 27
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 9](#1295161-1312188){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295161-1312188.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16.5-20141031.html "Link to previous version of section 16.5"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Minor Injury Record]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909844 .Subheading}

- **[[16.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall keep a record
  of each minor injury of which he is aware that affected any of his
  employees in the course of employment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A record made pursuant to subsection (1) shall
  contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date, time and location of the occurrence that
    resulted in the minor injury;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the injured or ill employee;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a brief description of the minor injury; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the causes of the minor injury.

### [Annual Report]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909852 .Subheading}

- **[[16.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall, not later than
  March 1 in each year, submit to the Head of Compliance and Enforcement
  a written report setting out the number of accidents, occupational
  illnesses and other hazardous occurrences of which the employer is
  aware that affected any of the employees of the employer in the course
  of employment during the 12 month period ending December 31 in the
  preceding year.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The report referred to in subsection (1) shall be in
  the form set out in Schedule II to this Part and contain the
  information required by the form.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 64
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 9](#1295161-1312190){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295161-1312190.html"}
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 27(E)](#1520620-1525134){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520620-1525134.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16.7-20210701.html "Link to previous version of section 16.7"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Retention of Reports and Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909858 .Subheading}

- **[[16.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), every
  employer shall keep a copy of each report and record referred to in
  this Part for one year after the day on which it was submitted to the
  Head of Compliance and Enforcement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every record with respect to a result referred to in
  paragraph 16.4(1)(f) shall be kept by the employer for a period of
  five years after the hazardous occurrence.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 65(F)
- SOR/2014-148, s. 28
- [SOR/2021-118, s. 9](#1295161-1312192){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1295161-1312192.html"}
:::

## [PART XVII]{.HLabel1}[First Aid]{.HTitleText1} {#h-909879 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909880 .Subheading}

**[[17.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,

[[first aid station]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[first aid station]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a place, other than
    a first aid room or medical clinic, in which first aid supplies or
    equipment are stored; ([poste de secours]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[isolated work place]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[isolated work place]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a work place that
    is more than two hours' travel time from a hospital or a medical
    clinic under normal travel conditions using the fastest available
    means of transportation; ([lieu de travail isolé]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[medical clinic]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[medical clinic]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a medical consultation
    and treatment facility that is in the charge of a medic or a
    physician. ([service de santé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 13(E)
:::

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909887 .Subheading}

- **[[17.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall establish and
  keep up-to-date written instructions that provide for the prompt
  rendering of first aid to an employee for any injury or illness,
  including an occupational illness.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the instructions referred to in
  subsection (1) shall be kept by the employer readily available for
  examination by employees.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employee, on sustaining an injury or becoming
  aware that they have contracted an illness, including an occupational
  illness, shall report as soon as practicable for treatment to a first
  aid attendant.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 24](#1520608-1525118){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520608-1525118.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.2-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17.2"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Physicians and First Aid Attendants]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909892 .Subheading}

**[[17.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.3 .sectionLabel}** A physician who has
specialized knowledge in the treatment of the safety and health problems
that may be encountered in the oil and gas industry shall be available
at all times for medical consultation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 14(E)
- SOR/94-165, s. 67(F)
- SOR/2014-141, s. 10
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17.3"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[17.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When there are not more than five
  employees normally working in a work place, other than an isolated
  work place, a first aid attendant shall be available.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} At an isolated work place in which not more than five
  employees are normally working, one of those employees shall be a
  first aid attendant who holds at least a standard first aid
  certificate.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-141, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.4-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17.4"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[17.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  a work place onshore in which the number of employees set out in
  Column I of an item of Schedule I to this Part is normally working,
  that number shall include the number of first aid attendants set out
  in Columns II, III and IV of that item.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} At a work place offshore in which the number of
  employees set out in Column I of an item of Schedule II to this Part
  is normally working, that number shall include the number of first aid
  attendants set out in Columns II, III and IV of that item.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a physician is available in a work place, the
  requirements of subsections (1) and (2) respecting the presence of a
  medic do not apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 15(E)
:::

- **[[17.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  addition to the requirements of section 17.5, when there are more than
  30 employees and fewer than 61 employees normally working in an
  isolated work place

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a medic, who may be one of the employees, shall, to
    the extent possible, be available in the work place; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when it is not possible for a medic to be available
    in the work place, the employer shall make arrangements to have a
    medic available at all times

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for consultation, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to be transported to the work place.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a physician is available in an isolated work
  place, the requirements of subsection (1) do not apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 16(E)
- SOR/94-165, s. 68(F)
- SOR/2014-141, s. 12
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.6-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17.6"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[17.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.7 .sectionLabel}** In addition to the
requirements of sections 17.4 to 17.6, at a work place in which any
employee is working on live high voltage electrical equipment, one of
the employees shall be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a first aid attendant who has successfully completed
  a CPR course; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} trained to provide

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} mouth-to-mouth resuscitation, cardiopulmonary
    resuscitation or any other direct resuscitation method, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} resuscitation by the Holger-Nielson Method,
    Sylvester Method or other similar method.

**[[17.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.8 .sectionLabel}** A first aid attendant
referred to in subsection 17.4(2), section 17.5 or 17.6 or paragraph
17.7(a) shall not be assigned duties that will interfere with the prompt
and adequate rendering of first aid and shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} be assigned to a first aid station or first aid room;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} be available at all times to employees in the work
  place; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} render first aid to employees that are injured or ill
  at the work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2014-141, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.8-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17.8"){.PITLink}
:::

### [First Aid Stations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909927 .Subheading}

- **[[17.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  least one first aid station shall be provided for every work place.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A first aid station shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} readily available to all employees; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} clearly identified by a conspicuous sign.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A first aid station shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} contain only supplies and equipment that are
    required for rendering first aid; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be inspected regularly and its contents maintained
    in a clean, dry and serviceable condition.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply where a first aid room
  or a medical clinic that meets the requirements of subsection (2) is
  provided by the employer.

### [Posting of Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909937 .Subheading}

- **[[17.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.10
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), an
  employer shall post and keep posted in a conspicuous place accessible
  to every employee in each work place

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} information regarding first aid to be rendered for
    any injury or illness, including an occupational illness, likely to
    be sustained or contracted in the work place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} information regarding the location of first aid
    attendants, first aid stations and first aid rooms; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} at every telephone, an up to date list of telephone
    numbers for use in emergencies.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} At an isolated work place or in a motor vehicle, the
  information referred to in subsection (1) shall be provided and kept
  with the first aid kit.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 25](#1520613-1525121){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520613-1525121.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.10-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17.10"){.PITLink}
:::

### [First Aid Supplies and Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909944 .Subheading}

- **[[17.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For each work place at which the
  number of employees working at any time is the number set out in
  Column I of an item of Schedule III to this Part, a first aid kit that
  is of the type set out in Column II of that item shall be provided.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), a first aid kit
  of a type set out at the head of Column II, III, IV, V or VI of
  Schedule IV to this Part shall contain the first aid supplies and
  equipment set out in Column I of an item of that Schedule in the
  applicable number set out opposite those supplies and equipment in
  Column II, III, IV, V or VI of that item.

<!-- -->

- **[[17.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), where a
  hazard for skin or eye injury from a hazardous substance exists in the
  work place, shower facilities to wash the skin and eye wash facilities
  to irrigate the eyes shall be provided for immediate use by employees.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where it is not practicable to comply with subsection
  (1), portable equipment that may be used in place of the facilities
  referred to in subsection (1) shall be provided.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 19
- SOR/94-165, s. 69(F)
- SOR/2014-141, s. 14(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.12-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17.12"){.PITLink}
:::

### [First Aid Rooms]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909953 .Subheading}

- **[[17.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A first aid room shall be provided

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where 60 or more employees are working at any time
    in a work place other than an isolated work place; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where 30 or more employees are working at any time
    in an isolated work place.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(a) does not apply where a medical
  clinic or hospital at which medical treatment is provided without
  charge to employees is readily accessible.

**[[17.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.14 .sectionLabel}** Every first aid
room provided in accordance with section 17.13 shall be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} under the supervision of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case where a physician is available in the
    work place, the physician,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case where there is a medic and no
    physician available in the work place, the medic, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the first aid attendant
    available in the work place who is the holder of the highest level
    of first aid certificate;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} located as close as is practicable to the work place
  and within easy access to a

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} toilet room,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} telephone, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} list of telephone numbers for use in emergencies;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} constructed to allow for optimum ease of access to
  persons carrying a patient on a stretcher;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} maintained in an orderly and sanitary condition;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} clearly identified by a conspicuous sign;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} equipped with

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a lockable storage cupboard and a counter,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the first aid supplies and equipment set out in
    Column I of an item of Schedule V to this Part in the applicable
    quantities set out in Column II of that item of that Schedule,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the emergency procedures referred to in
    section 18.9 of Part XVIII, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} information regarding hazardous substances in the
    work place and the first aid required to treat exposure to the
    hazardous substances; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} maintained, where practicable, at a temperature of
  not less than 18°C and not more than 24°C, measured 1 m above the
  floor.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, ss. 17(E), 19
- SOR/94-165, s. 70(E)
- SOR/2014-141, s. 14(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.14-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17.14"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Transportation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909979 .Subheading}

**[[17.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.15 .sectionLabel}** Before assigning
employees to a work place, the employer shall provide for that work
place

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an ambulance service or other suitable means of
  transporting an injured or ill employee

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where practicable, to a hospital at which a
    physician referred to in section 17.3 practises, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where it is not practicable to comply with
    subparagraph (i), to a medical clinic in the charge of a medic who
    is in contact with a physician referred to in section 17.3;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a first aid attendant to accompany an injured or ill
  employee and to render first aid in transit, if required; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a means of quickly summoning the ambulance service or
  other means of transportation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/88-199, s. 18(E)
:::

### [Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-909988 .Subheading}

- **[[17.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an injured or ill employee
  reports for treatment to a first aid attendant in accordance with
  subsection 17.2(3) or where a first aid attendant renders first aid to
  an employee, the first aid attendant shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} enter in a first aid record the following
    information:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date and time that the injury or illness,
      including an occupational illness, was reported,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the full name of the injured or ill employee,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the date, time and site of the occurrence of
      the injury or illness, including an occupational illness,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a brief description of the injury or illness,
      including an occupational illness,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a brief description of the first aid rendered, if
      any, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a brief description of the arrangements made for
      the treatment or transportation of the injured or ill employee, if
      any; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sign the first aid record adjacent to the
    information entered in accordance with paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall keep a first aid record containing
  information entered in accordance with subsection (1) for one year
  after the date of that entry.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 71(F)
- [SOR/2025-79, s. 26](#1520615-1525125){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1520615-1525125.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17.16-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17.16"){.PITLink}
:::

::::: section
:::: {#910002 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [SCHEDULE I]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subsection 17.5(1))]{.OriginatingRef} {#h-910003 .scheduleLabel}
:::

<figure id="910004">
<table
class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
<thead>
<tr class="topdouble">
<th rowspan="2" class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Item</th>

I</th>

II</th>

## [PART XVIII]{.HLabel1}[Safe Occupancy of the Work Place]{.HTitleText1} {#h-910044 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-910045 .Subheading}

**[[18.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.1 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[emergency evacuation plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a written plan
for use in an emergency, prepared in accordance with section 18.12.
([*plan d'évacuation d'urgence*]{.otherLang lang="fr"})

### [Fire Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-910047 .Subheading}

**[[18.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.2 .sectionLabel}** Every work place
shall be so designed, constructed and arranged as to minimize, so far as
is reasonably practicable, the risk of fire.

- **[[18.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Fire escapes, exits, stairways and
  any other means of evacuation at a work place shall be in serviceable
  condition and ready for use at all times.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Exits to the exterior shall be clearly identified by
  signs.

### [Fire Hazard Areas]{.HTitleText2} {#h-910052 .Subheading}

- **[[18.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall, in a fire hazard area,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (2), perform any hot work;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} smoke; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} use an open flame or other source of ignition.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where it is not practicable to avoid performing hot
  work in a fire hazard area, the employer shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} issue written instructions with respect to the
    procedures to be followed that will provide for the safe performance
    of that work;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} show and explain the instructions referred to in
    paragraph (a) to any employee who is required to work in the fire
    hazard area; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} keep a copy of the instructions referred to in
    paragraph (a) readily available for examination by employees.

**[[18.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.5 .sectionLabel}** Signs shall be posted
in conspicuous places at all entrances to a fire hazard area

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} identifying the area as a fire hazard area; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} prohibiting the use of an open flame or other source
  of ignition in the fire hazard area.

### [Alarm Systems]{.HTitleText2} {#h-910065 .Subheading}

**[[18.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.6 .sectionLabel}** Every work place
shall be equipped with an alarm system that warns all employees when

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the safety of the work place is threatened;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} employees are to be evacuated from the work place;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a fire is likely to threaten the safety or health of
  employees at the work place; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} there is a malfunction of a mechanical ventilation
  system provided for an area where concentrations of toxic or
  combustible gases may accumulate.

### [Emergency Electrical Power]{.HTitleText2} {#h-910071 .Subheading}

**[[18.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.7 .sectionLabel}** Every drilling rig,
drilling unit and production facility shall be equipped with an
emergency electrical power supply sufficient to operate, for at least 18
consecutive hours,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} alarm system and warning devices;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} emergency lighting referred to in section 7.5;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} internal and external communications systems; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} light and sound signals marking the location of the
  work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 73
:::

**[[18.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.8 .sectionLabel}** Where an emergency
switchboard is provided, it shall be independent of the main electrical
power supply and shall be located as near as is reasonably practicable
to the emergency electrical power supply.

### [Emergency Procedures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-910080 .Subheading}

- **[[18.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall prepare
  emergency procedures to be implemented

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if any person commits or threatens to commit an
    act, other than an occurrence of harassment and violence, that is
    likely to be hazardous to the safety or health of the employer or
    any employee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when a hazardous occurrence referred to in
    subsection 16.4(1) occurs;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where evacuation is not an appropriate means of
    ensuring the safety and health of employees; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the event of a failure of the lighting system.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where two or more employers are engaged in work at
  the same work place, those employers shall prepare common emergency
  procedures.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the emergency procedures referred to in
  subsection (1) or (2) shall be kept up to date and readily accessible
  to all employees at the work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 74
- [SOR/2020-130, s. 47](#1251043-1282809){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1251043-1282809.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18.9-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 18.9"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[18.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.10 .sectionLabel}** The emergency
procedures referred to in section 18.9 shall contain a full written
description of the procedures to be followed by the employees, including

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the duties of the employees during the execution of
  the procedures;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name, position, usual location and telephone
  number of each person responsible for the execution of the procedures;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a list of agencies, companies or organizations that
  could render assistance in the event of an emergency and their
  telephone numbers; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a list of the emergency and protection equipment
  required to carry out the procedures.

### [Emergency Evacuation Plan]{.HTitleText2} {#h-910096 .Subheading}

**[[18.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.11 .sectionLabel}** Where the emergency
procedures referred to in section 18.9 provide for the evacuation of
employees from a work place, an emergency evacuation plan shall be
prepared by the employer or employers.

**[[18.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.12 .sectionLabel}** The emergency
evacuation plan referred to in section 18.11 shall include

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a general layout plan and elevation drawing of the
  buildings or structures at a work place, including the date and scale
  of the drawing and the name of the person who verified the drawing;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name, address and telephone number of the owner
  or owners of the buildings or structures where the work place is
  located and a list of the tenants, if any;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the relative location of other buildings, structures
  or streets within 30 m of the boundaries of the work place;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a statement of the maximum number of people who can
  safely occupy the work place under normal conditions;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a drawing illustrating the arrangement of each level
  of the buildings or structures at the work place that will clearly
  show

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the location of all exits, stairways, elevators,
    corridors, fire escapes and any other routes of exit,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the location, quantity and type of emergency and
    protection equipment,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the location of the main emergency shut-down
    switches for the lighting, heating, ventilation, air conditioning
    and elevator systems and other electrical equipment,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the location, quantity and type of all
    communications equipment,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the location, number, type, size and capacity of
    any support craft or other means of transport to be used to evacuate
    the work place, and

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the location of first aid areas and casualty
    clearing areas; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the estimated amount of time required to complete the
  execution of the plan under normal conditions.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 75(E)
:::

### [Instructions and Training]{.HTitleText2} {#h-910113 .Subheading}

- **[[18.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee shall be instructed
  and trained in

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to be followed by him in the event
    of an emergency; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the location, use and operation of emergency and
    fire protection equipment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A record of all training provided to an employee in
  accordance with subsection (1) shall be kept by the employer for as
  long as the employee remains in his employ.

### [Emergency Drills]{.HTitleText2} {#h-910119 .Subheading}

- **[[18.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A fire drill shall be conducted at
  least once

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} every two weeks at each drilling rig, drilling unit
    and production facility; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} every 12 months at every work place other than a
    work place referred to in paragraph (a).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An evacuation drill shall be conducted at least once

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} every week at a drilling unit and an offshore
    production facility; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} every 12 months at a work place other than a work
    place referred to in paragraph (a).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the drills referred to in
  subsections (1) and (2), a fire drill and an evacuation drill shall be
  conducted

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prior to the commencement of workover, completion,
    recompletion or stimulation of a well; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after any significant change is made in the
    emergency procedures or emergency evacuation plan.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A blowout prevention drill shall be conducted at
  least once each week that the blowout preventer is in use.

### [Standby Craft]{.HTitleText2} {#h-910131 .Subheading}

**[[18.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.15 .sectionLabel}** For every drilling
operation and production operation, the employer shall provide a standby
craft capable of safely evacuating all employees from the work place.

### [Condition of Employees]{.HTitleText2} {#h-910133 .Subheading}

**[[18.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.16 .sectionLabel}** No employee shall
work where that employee's ability to function is impaired as a result
of fatigue, illness, alcohol, drugs or any other condition that may be
hazardous to the safety or health of any employee at the work place.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/94-165, s. 76(F)
:::

**[[18.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.17 .sectionLabel}** Section 18.16 does
not apply in the event of an emergency at the work place that may be
hazardous to the safety or health of employees.

### [Notices and Records]{.HTitleText2} {#h-910138 .Subheading}

- **[[18.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notices shall be posted at
  appropriate locations at a work place setting out the emergency
  procedures to be followed and the escape routes to be used in the
  event of an emergency.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall keep a record of all emergency
  drills and evacuation drills carried out by his employees for one year
  after the drill.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The record referred to in subsection (2) shall
  contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date and time at which the drill was conducted;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the length of time taken by the employees to
    complete the drill.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the emergency procedures and emergency
  evacuation plan prepared for the work place shall be kept readily
  available for examination by employees.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall keep a daily record of each
  employee present at the work place and of each person granted access
  to the work place.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} The record referred to in subsection (5) shall
  contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the employee or the person granted
    access to the work place; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name of the employer.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} The record referred to in subsection (5) shall be
  kept by the employer for two months after the date of the last daily
  entry made therein.

::: ScheduleRP
## RELATED PROVISIONS {#related-provisions .scheduleTitleText}

- --- SOR/2016-141, s. 77

  - [77]{.lawlabel} If an employer complies with the requirements set
    out in the provisions amended by these Regulations as those
    provisions read immediately before February 11, 2015, the amendments
    made by these Regulations do not apply to the employer

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} during the period that begins on the day on which
      these Regulations come into force and ends on November 30, 2018;
      and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of any hazardous products that are in
      the work place on December 1, 2018, during the period that begins
      on December 1, 2018 and ends on May 31, 2019.
:::
